# DIVERGENCES

This file is generated by `make divergences` from the one-file-per-record sources in `docs/parity/divergences/` (`D<N>.md`). Edit those files only; a lane does not commit this file, the merger regenerates it with `make divergences`. An active record is a `## D<N> <title>` section; a retired record is one `- D<N>: ...` line.

Only user visible or interop relevant differences from upstream belong here.

Do not log:

- mechanical TS to Go translation
- test harness differences
- multi language SDK surface differences over the same wire protocol

Every active divergence must have:

- id `D<N>`
- `// pig divergence (D<N>): ...` at each call site
- parity coverage or an explicit parity allowance
- `SCRUTINIZED:approved`
- if a test-mapping entry names it, the labeled paragraphs `Protects:`, `PiG-shaped test considered:` and `Why not:` (see AGENTS.md, Upstream test parity)

## Retired divergences

- D35: Hidden `/arminsayshi` and `/dementedelves` commands. Retired by the Pi component ports (`armin.ts` and `earendil-announcement.ts` are identical in upstream 0.87.1 through 1.0.0). The commands, their recognition and `/dementedelves`'s announcement remain Pi's. Since 2026-10-01, D87 replaces the art and label `/arminsayshi` shows with PiG's pig head and `pigsayhi`, keeps Pi's effects, and adds `/pigsayhi`. `TestArminFramesMatchPinnedPi` runs the pinned `armin.ts` with PiG's image and compares every frame of all seven effects, and `TestEarendilAnnouncementMatchesPinnedPi` compares styled announcement rows. The `slash-commands/12-earendil-announcement` scenario compares exact terminal output; `13-armin-bitmap` asserts each binary's settled image. The ID remains reserved.

- D47: Width stripping consumes DEC private-mode set/reset sequences. Retired by the width-parity change. `tui/widthx.ExtractAnsi` now delegates to the upstream-compatible `ExtractAnsiCode`; the ID remains reserved. `TestExtractAnsi_PrivateModeMatchesUpstream` and `TestPiWidthDifferential` verify the shared ANSI parsing behavior. No active divergence or source marker remains.

- D50: Extra unsupported/oversized Mermaid hints. Retired under the lead's parity-completion authority. upstream 0.99.1 `mermaid.ts:76-77` (unchanged since 0.87.1) preserves the raw code block; partial-parse warnings remain display-only. `TestMermaidUnsupportedAndOversizedRemainSource`, `TestMermaidFallbackAssistantBlockMatchesDisabledTransform`, and the now byte-exact `TestMermaidTransformMatchesUpstream` verify the contract. The ID remains reserved.

- D54: Fenced-code wrapping. Retired after re-probing Pi: `Markdown.render` already wraps every non-image rendered row, including code rows (`markdown.ts` at 0.99.1 still passes each non-image line through `wrapTextWithAnsi`; the only change since 0.87.1 is a token cache). PiG now uses that same final content-width pass and its continuation breakpoints. The ID remains reserved. Evidence: `tui/markdown_upstream_test.go`, `tui/markdown_codeblock_wrap_test.go`, and `test/parity/scenarios/tui-components/16-markdown-user-components.toml`.

- D58: Automatic Mermaid label narrowing. Retired under the same authority. The transformer uses the natural layout and preserves source when it is too wide. The caller-free fitting search and its private bookkeeping are removed. The oversized transformer/caller cases retain the wide diagram input, and the natural engine corpus remains unchanged. The ID remains reserved.

- D59: Generic extension tool cards exposed complete recoverable details. Retired by the upstream 0.99.1 port: `formatToolCallWithArgs` (`render-utils.ts:71-96`) now draws the fallback call header of a registered tool without `renderCall`, as `key=value` pairs cut at 100 characters while collapsed and one `key: value` line per argument when expanded (`tool-execution.ts:155-157`). PiG uses that function and the definition card path for every registered tool; the structured-argument card, its width-aware preview, the extra Ctrl+O scrollback rebuild and the "Toggle tool details" key description are removed, so Ctrl+O is "Toggle tool output" again. Tests: `TestFormatToolCallWithArgs`, `TestToolExecutionComponentUpstream/shows_arguments_in_the_fallback_call_header` and `TestRegisteredToolWithoutRenderersDrawsTheFallbackHeaderThroughTheEventPath`. The ID remains reserved.

- D71: Nonfatal main-screen overflow recovery. Withdrawn on 2026-09-25 by owner decision; the ID remains reserved. PiG now matches upstream `tui-main-screen.ts`: an over-wide non-image row that reaches the differential-render loop writes the TUI crash log (`pi-tui-crash.log` in the configured agent directory, or the OS temp directory when none is configured), stops the TUI, and ends the process through the uncaught-exception path with status 1. Initial, full, and resize renders emit the row unchanged. Tests: `tui/render_overflow_test.go` and parity scenario `extensions-runtime/20-differential-render-overflow-terminates.toml`. No active divergence or source marker remains.

- D76: Untransformed Markdown before an extension reply. Retired by the off-loop transform-generation path. The complete ordered chain finishes before new Markdown content is painted. Each component retains at most one active and one replaceable pending generation; cancellation and stale-result rejection keep replacements from publishing late. Tests: `TestMarkdownTransformFirstPaintWaitsForWholeChain`, `TestAsyncMarkdownReplacesPendingGeneration`, `TestAsyncMarkdownOwnerCancellationDrains`, and `extensions-runtime/33-markdown-transformer`. D56 still governs failed or stalled subprocess rendering.

- D77: Explicit Node isolation prevented sharing `pi.events`. Retired by owner decision Q1 = B (2026-09-29). Every Node process is one cross-process reference realm, and every realm shares the one bus: packed cells, crash-recovery groups, quarantined members, strict isolation and exact standalones. Payloads cross by reference, so listeners in other processes see the emitter's original object; D83 records the remaining cross-realm scheduling and lifetime boundaries. Process, crash and memory isolation remain; a hung foreign listener blocks its emitter, as a hung listener blocks Pi. Tests: `TestXrefEventBusForeignPrefixMutationMatchesPi`, `TestXrefEventBusMatchesPiAcrossRealms`, `TestXrefEventBusListenerOrderMatchesPi` and `TestXrefEventBusReentrantDispatchMatchesPi` run strict isolation, packed and mixed topologies against Pi's own `createEventBus`. The ID remains reserved.

- D119: An MCP OAuth `iss` parameter is rejected when no authorization server metadata was discovered. Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main c5f5b328 (`packages/mcp/src/oauth/flow.ts` `runFlow`, `errors.ts` `OAuthIssuerMismatchError`) rejects such an `iss` with `OAuthIssuerMismatchError` (expected none, received the `iss`), and `mcp/oauth` matches it. `TestMCPOAuthExchangesACodeOnlyWhenItsIssParameterNamesTheAuthorizationServer` ports Pi's `test/oauth.test.ts` case of the same name and `TestMCPOAuthIssMismatchWithoutMetadataHasNoExpectedIssuer` covers the `expected none` error. The ID remains reserved.

- D124: OpenRouter's reported cost is the usage total, ported from pi-mono commit 83ca8020 (pi-mono #9980). Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main cae8d69e5 (#10286, `packages/ai/src/utils/openrouter-cost.ts`) ships the same rule: for an OpenRouter model the OpenAI Completions usage chunk and the Anthropic Messages `message_delta` usage replace `usage.cost.total` with the reported `cost`, or with `cost` plus the upstream inference cost for a BYOK request, and set `usage.cost.totalFromProvider`. `ai/openrouter_cost.go` is that file's port, `internal/codingagent/export/assets/template.js` is Pi's file (its hash is pinned by `TestExportHTMLPinnedAssets`), and the export-html scenario rules that rewrote the template hunks are gone. `TestApplyOpenRouterReportedCost`, `TestOpenAICompletionsUsesOpenRouterReportedCost`, `TestAnthropicMessagesUsesOpenRouterReportedCost` and `TestExportHTMLCostUsesRecordedTotals` port Pi's tests. The ID remains reserved.

- D125: Anthropic strict "prefer" tools fall back when the provider rejects the strict tool set. Retired by the Pi 1.4.0 pin: Pi #10409 (`packages/ai/src/api/anthropic-messages.ts:644-689` in `stream`, `buildParams` `disablePreferredStrictTools`, `convertTools`) retries once with `prefer` tools non-strict when Anthropic rejects the strict tool set for grammar size, tool count or union limits, keeps `require` tools strict and appends the `anthropic_strict_tools_fallback` diagnostic. `ai/anthropic.go` ports it; `TestAnthropicPreferredStrictToolFallbackUpstream` ports Pi's `anthropic-strict-tool-fallback.test.ts`. The ID remains reserved.

- D126: Another model's thinking replays as `reasoning_content` on targets that require it (pi-mono #6167). Retired by Pi commit `91130942` (#11000, #10995; in Pi 1.4.0): `transformMessages` (`packages/ai/src/api/transform-messages.ts:129-137`) drops the thinking of another model for every target instead of inlining it as assistant text, so no target receives it. PiG follows Pi (owner decision 2026-10-16): `ai.TransformMessages` and `agent.normalizeAssistantReplayBlocks` drop it, and a target that requires `reasoning_content` gets the empty placeholder, as in Pi. The D126 code (`keepForeignThinking`, the unnamed-signature branch of `convertMessagesWithCompat`, `providerReplaysForeignThinking`), its tests and the oracle accommodations (`foldForeignReasoning`, the D126 probe skip and generator rule) are removed.

- D127: The Mistral request timeout covers only the wait for response headers. Retired by the Pi pin `1.1.0+42a3497d`: Pi commit `6b07b4e5` (pi-mono #10609) is in the pinned source and `ai/mistral.go` matches it (`TestMistralHTTPTransportUpstream`).

- D133: A leading `~` in a resource pattern expands to the home directory. Retired by the Pi 1.4.0 pin: Pi #10765 passes every pattern through `normalizePath` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/package-manager.ts:678` `matchesAnyPattern`, `:695-697` `normalizeExactPattern`), and `coding/packagecontent` (`normalizePatternPath`, `normalizeExactPattern`) does the same, file URLs and Windows shell paths included. `TestPackagePatternsExpandTildeUpstream` ports Pi's "should expand ~ in exclusion and exact patterns"; `TestPatternsAreNormalizedLikePaths` covers the other `normalizePath` forms. The ID remains reserved.

- D139: A user-prompt run did not count as busy while it started (residual of Pi #10739). Retired by the Pi 1.4.0 pin: `agent-session.ts:1826-1936` (`_startRun`, `_prepareRun`) and `:1104-1106` (`_handleAgentEvent`'s `agent_start` abort) are ported for both triggers. `coding.RunStart` marks the Session busy before the compaction check and `before_agent_start` (`Session.prepareContentRun`, `sendCustomMessage`, RPC prompt admission), held bash results and custom messages are appended after the handlers, a prompt or `sendUserMessage` must name its delivery meanwhile, an abort aborts the run at `agent_start`, and `Runner.EmitBeforeAgentStart` takes Pi's `(trigger, systemPromptOptions)`. `TestUpstreamSessionPromptRunsStartedByACustomMessage` ports `agent-session-prompt.test.ts:566-721`; `TestUserPromptRunIsBusyWhileItStarts` covers the user trigger. The ID remains reserved.

- D141: Thinking-only messages were truncated in compaction summaries (Pi #9602). Retired by Pi commit `15e7bdc1`, which omits thinking from `serializeConversation`; `compaction.SerializeConversation` does the same (`TestSerializeConversationUpstream` "should omit thinking", `TestRPCCompactionSurvivesThinkingOnlyMessagesTheRequestsOmit`).

- D151: `grep` with context reads a window of each matched file (Pi #9276). Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main 8da141c2a (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/grep.ts`) takes the context lines from ripgrep and no longer reads or caches a matched file, so neither Pi nor PiG loads a whole file for `context`; `GrepOperations.readFile` is gone from Pi and from every SDK. `TestGrepPrintsOverlappingContextOnceAndKeepsTheLastMatchsTrailingContext` and its neighbours (`internal/codingagent/tools/grep_ripgrep_context_test.go`) port Pi's tests of the change. The ID remains reserved.

- D152: codemode joins adjacent text items in one pass. Retired by the Pi 1.4.0 pin: Pi #10678 (`packages/coding-agent/src/extensions/codemode/execute.ts:293-317` `joinAdjacentText`) collects a run of text items in a parts array, separated by a newline unless the text so far is empty or ends with one, and joins it once; `coding/extension/builtin/codemode/execute.go` `joinAdjacentText` does the same in a `strings.Builder` (`TestJoinAdjacentTextIsLinearInTheNumberOfItems`, `TestJoinAdjacentTextMatchesUpstreamOnMixedItems`). The ID remains reserved.

- D154: The read header adds a string offset and limit as numbers (Pi #9887). Retired by the Pi pin `1.2.0+b06c1f2c`: Pi commit `91014991` (#10938) adds `formatReadLineRange` to `packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/render-utils.ts`, which reads finite numbers and numeric strings and drops a malformed range, and uses it in the read header, the session tree and the HTML export template. `tui.FormatReadLineRange` ports it; the export embeds Pi's template unpatched; `TestFormatReadLineRangeMatchesPi` and `TestExportedViewerFormatsReadLineRangeLikePi` port Pi's `read-line-range-display.test.ts`. The ID remains reserved.

- D166: A resumed session takes its active tools from the transcript. Retired by Pi 6f2f164a (#10887, fixes #10785): the `AgentSession` constructor restores the transcript's loadout whenever the session uses the default tools (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:526`), with the `--tools` `+name`/`-name` entries applied (`_restoreToolsFromTranscript(modifiers)`), and `coding/session.go` `NewSession` does the same (`restoreToolsFromTranscript(opts.DefaultToolModifiers...)`). `TestResumeRestoresTheRecordedLoadoutPort` ports Pi's `default-tools-setting.test.ts` describe("resume"); `TestRPCResumeKeepsDynamicallyActivatedTools` and `TestResumeRestoresTheTranscriptsActiveTools` keep covering the resume. The ID remains reserved.

- D171: An errored Anthropic tool result carries no images (Pi #10792, #10794; Pi 34a8bb38). Retired by the Pi pin `1.2.0+5ab7fe74`: Pi commit `34a8bb38` replaced each image of an error tool result with a text note, and Pi commit `5ab7fe74` replaces it with a placeholder and sends the images after all tool results of the turn, in the same user message, under a caption naming the tool call (`splitErrorToolResultImages` in `packages/ai/src/api/transform-messages.ts`, used by `anthropic-messages.ts` and, for Claude, `bedrock-converse-stream.ts`). `ai.SplitErrorToolResultImages` and the Anthropic and Bedrock converters do the same, and the payload oracles no longer project Pi's payload.

- D173: Tool search matches Chinese, Japanese and Korean queries (Pi #10816). Retired by Pi 79006f06 (#11037, fixes #10816), which D217 carries: Pi's `tokenize` (`packages/coding-agent/src/extensions/tool-search/tool.ts`, `packages/durable/src/search.ts`) NFC-normalizes the text, reduces it to letters, marks and numbers and splits it with `Intl.Segmenter` at word granularity, so words in every script match and `搜索记录` is split into words by ICU's dictionary. `internal/searchtext.Tokenize` does the same with `internal/wordsegmenter` (ICU 78.3 word rules and the CJK, Thai, Lao, Khmer and Burmese dictionaries, which the editor already embeds) and `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm`; PiG no longer makes each character a term. `TestTokenizeMatchesPiOnEveryScriptClass` (`coding/extension/builtin/toolsearch`, `durable/internal/search`) compares 4,854 Pi 79006f06 tokenizations of every script class with no difference, and `TestTokenizeSegmentsUnicodeWords` and `TestBm25RankerMatchesUnicodeQueries` port Pi's tests. A Piglet Binary built with `pig_strip_word_dictionaries` has no dictionary, so it keeps a run of Han, Hiragana, Katakana, Thai, Lao, Khmer or Myanmar text as one term (D92, `TestTokenizeKeepsDictionaryScriptRunsWholeWithoutDictionaries`). The ID remains reserved.

- D188: Opening a store at an older Durable schema version keeps a copy of it first, where Pi upgrades it in place. Retired by Pi 1.4.0 (npm `@earendil-works/pi-durable` 1.4.0, tag `v1.4.0` at `4a080252`, 2026-10-21), which writes SQLite schema version 2: Pi commit `f75b3240` (feat(durable): durable sleeping state replaces runtime.sleep, `packages/durable/src/storage/sqlite/migrations.ts:91-116`) adds the `sleeping` task status as migration 2, and the pin `1.4.0+b3a29f11` carries it unchanged. A user going back to a released Pi can open an upgraded store, so the copy PiG wrote to `<path>.schema-1.bak` is no longer needed. `durable/internal/schemabackup` and the two call-site markers are gone; `driver.OpenHarness` and `node.OpenNodeSqliteStorage` upgrade a version 1 store in place, as Pi does (`TestOpenHarnessUpgradesAVersionOneStoreInPlace`, `TestOpenNodeSqliteStorageUpgradesAVersionOneStoreInPlace`; Pi's migration test is twinned by `durable/storage/sqlite/sqlite_migrations_test.go`). Pi releases before 1.4.0 still refuse a version 2 store; a user who needs one keeps their own copy.

- D217: Pi bug fixes after the pin that next carried (Pi e7a0df27..e3da248e). Retired by pin 2c8d1feb, which is at or after every carried Pi commit (`86dc686c` through `e3da248e`); its fixes are Pi behavior, and the markers, oracle hooks and allowances are gone.

- D231: RPC mode acts on an idle `ctx.shutdown()`. Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main 1708b4788 (`rpc-mode.ts` `shutdownHandler`, `checkShutdownRequested`, #11746, fixing #11599) is in the pinned Pi (c23b9ec1). A re-probe of the pin's `rpc-mode.ts` shows the same gate (the request, input attached, no command in flight, session idle; checked on the next tick, after a command and at `agent_settled`), and `TestRPCExtensionShutdownRequestExits` and `TestRPCShutdownAfterSynchronousUIEffectKeepsPiOrder` compare the pinned Pi with PiG, which writes the response before the notification as Pi does. `TestRPCExtensionShutdownWhileIdleExitsWithoutAnotherCommand`, `TestRPCExtensionShutdownFromSessionStartExits`, `TestRPCExtensionCommandShutdownWritesResponseBeforeShutdown`, `TestRPCExtensionShutdownWaitsForCommandOfSameChunk`, `TestRPCShutdownGate` and `TestRPCGoSDKIdleShutdownReturnsBeforeSessionShutdown` (isolated and packed) cover it. The ID remains reserved.

- D233: A Streamable HTTP application/json reply is capped at maxMessageBytes, and an HTTP error body keeps its first 8192 bytes. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 4a8b60bc3 (fix(mcp): apply maxMessageBytes to Streamable HTTP JSON responses, #11749): the pinned Pi reads an `application/json` reply through `readBodyText` and rejects one over `maxMessageBytes` with `MCP JSON response exceeds N bytes`, and keeps only the first 8192 bytes of an HTTP error body (`MAX_ERROR_BODY_BYTES`), which is what `readBodyBytes` and `checkResponse` do, so the two call-site markers are gone. `TestStreamableHTTPAppliesMaxMessageBytesToJSONResponses`, `TestStreamableHTTPDecodesJSONResponsesSplitInsideABOMAndAMultiByteCharacter`, `TestStreamableHTTPReadsOnlyAnExcerptOfHTTPErrorBodiesAndCancelsTheRest` and `TestStreamableHTTPTransportKeepsTheFirstMaxErrorBodyBytesOfAnErrorBody` hold the behavior.

- D234: A started RPC or print shutdown finishes, and only a repeated signal forces exit. Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main ace560c30 (`rpc-mode.ts` `shutdown` and its signal handlers, `print-mode.ts` `disposeRuntime`, #11752, fixing #11599) is in the pinned Pi (c23b9ec1). A re-probe of the pin shows the signal handlers stay until exit, only a repeated SIGTERM/SIGHUP calls `process.exit`, and print mode keeps its handlers through the final disposal. `TestRPCShutdownTriggers`, `TestRPCSingleSignalDuringInputEndShutdownLetsItFinish`, `TestRPCInputEndDuringSignalShutdownLetsItFinish`, `TestRPCRepeatedSignalDuringShutdownExitsAtOnce`, `TestPrintModeSignalDuringFinalDisposalWaitsForIt` and `TestPrintModeRepeatedSignalAfterStdoutFailureExitsAtOnce` cover it. What stays PiG's own: the `exit.log` line that names what ended the process (D102, additive), and the Go runtime limit that two termination signals delivered back to back can reach the process as one (a supervisor sends a forcing signal after a short delay). The ID remains reserved.

- D236: The auth lock follows the symlinks of auth.json to the real file. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 081a6e87f (fix(coding-agent): share the auth.json lock across symlinked paths, #11787): the pinned Pi no longer passes `realpath: false`, so the auth.json lock (and the models.json and mcp-auth.json locks that go through it) takes its directory next to the resolved file, as `internal/pilock` `Acquire` and `AcquireAuthSync` do, so the two call-site markers are gone and the lock-error oracle is handed the path as given. `TestAuthLocksShareOneLockAcrossSymlinkedPaths`, `TestAuthLockOfASymlinkedFileInAnotherDirectoryIsTheRealFilesLock`, `TestSettingsLockStaysOnTheGivenPath`, `TestAuthStorageCreatesTheTargetOfADanglingSymlinkedAuthFile` and `TestFileModelsStoreCreatesTheTargetOfADanglingSymlinkedFile` hold the behavior.

- D237: Exit waits for a started OAuth refresh to be saved. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi b2cb62923 (fix(coding-agent): wait for a started OAuth refresh to be saved before exit, #11812): the pinned Pi tracks every credential modification (`pendingModifications`), exposes `ModelRuntime.settleCredentialWrites` and has `AgentSessionRuntime.dispose` wait for it after `session.dispose()`, as `ai.RuntimeCredentials.Settled` and `coding.Runtime.Close` do, so the call-site markers are gone. PiG-only paths without a Pi counterpart (a subprocess extension's native provider refresh and request-time auth, which run Pi's `refreshStoredOAuthCredential` rule over the registry's credential overlay) keep their behavior; the standalone GitHub Copilot token manager stays untracked, as it writes back the unchanged long-lived token. `TestRuntimeCredentialsSettledWaitsUntilPendingModificationsAreWritten`, `TestRuntimeCredentialsSettledResolvesAfterAPendingModificationFails`, `TestRuntimeCloseWaitsUntilTheRotatedTokenOfAStartedRefreshIsSaved`, `TestRuntimeCloseWaitsForTheCatalogRefreshOfAnExpiredCredentialAfterItsContextEnded`, `TestNativeProviderCatalogRefreshSavesTheRotatedTokenAfterItsContextEnded` and `TestRuntimeCloseSettlesCredentialWritesBeforeItRetiresTheFactoryResources` hold the behavior.

- D238: The output limit leaves 15% of the estimated input unused. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 0a4fad548 (fix(ai): leave room for OpenRouter's input overcount when clamping max tokens, #11712): the pinned Pi's `clampMaxTokensToContext` leaves max(4096, 15% of the estimated input) as the margin and caps the limit at 32768 when the room is under that, as `ClampMaxTokensToContext` does, so the two call-site markers are gone. `TestClampMaxTokensToContextOpenRouterOvercountMargin` holds the behavior.

- D240: Claude Sonnet 5 takes no temperature. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 4b9777737 (fix(ai): mark Claude Sonnet 5 as not supporting temperature, #7961): the pinned Pi's family rule lists `sonnet-5` and `sonnet.5` as taking no temperature and its published provider data (the source of the generated catalogs and of the Node runtime's `pi-dist` data) carries `compat.supportsTemperature: false` on every Sonnet 5 record, so the generator step `applyD240SonnetFiveTemperature`, the parity allowances for the seven records and the family-rule marker are gone. `TestAnthropicTemperatureCompatibility/omits_zero_for_Claude_Sonnet_5`, `TestApplyClaudeFamilyRules/sonnet_5_drops_the_temperature`, `TestCatalogGenerationMatchesPi`, `TestGeneratedCatalogsMatchPublishedPackage`, `TestCatalogModelJSONMatchesPiForEveryBuiltinModel`, `TestBuiltinCatalogsMatchPinnedProviderDataModules` and `TestModelObjectsMatchPisModelObjects` hold the behavior.

- D241: Ctrl+V also pastes on Windows and WSL. Retired by the pin `1.5.0+c23b9ec1`, which contains Pi 37b38e9f6 (keep ctrl+v as a paste key on Windows and WSL, #11780): the Windows and WSL default of `app.clipboard.pasteImage` is `alt+v` and `ctrl+v` in the pinned Pi, so the call-site marker and the oracle allowances are gone. `TestKeybindingDefinitionsApplyPlatformDefaults` and `TestAppKeybindingDefinitionsMatchUpstreamInventory` hold the default.

- D242: Reload is rejected while the session is running or compacting. Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main c3b938e3e (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts` `reload`, #9222) rejects it the same way, with the same messages. `TestSessionReloadRejectedWhileRunningOrCompacting` and `TestRefusedReloadDuringARunningToolKeepsTheExtensionHostLive` cover it, and the scenario `reload-rejected-while-running-reaches-the-caller-unchanged` keeps the message identical in every SDK. The ID remains reserved.

- D243: A bash or PowerShell timeout after the shell already exited reports the shell's exit code. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 06aa6563e (fix(coding-agent): report the shell exit code when bash times out after the shell exited, #11813): the pinned Pi's `ShellTimeoutError` carries the shell's exit code and run time, and the bash tool says `The shell exited with code C after S seconds, but background processes it started kept writing output until the timeout.`, as the local shell backend's `*ShellTimeoutError` and the shell tool do, so the two call-site markers are gone. The Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs' `CreateLocalBashOperations` and `CreateLocalPowerShellOperations` carry the same: the `builtinTool.localExec` error reply and the SDK's `user_bash_exec` error response hold Pi's `shellExit` (`ErrorInfo.shellExit`), and the error an exec returns unchanged from the local operations, on whatever thread or task, makes the bash tool say the sentence, while an error wrapped around it or built with its message gives the plain timeout, as Pi's class check does. `TestBashTimeoutAfterShellExit11613` and the shell-exit cases of `TestConformance_BuiltinToolDelegationAcrossSDKs` hold the behavior.

- D244: A user bash result whose earlier output was discarded is reported as truncated. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 66c655677 (fix(coding-agent): track discarded user bash output, #10165): the pinned Pi's `executeBashWithOperations` records a discarded chunk and reports `truncated: true` for a result whose head the rolling window dropped, as `ExecuteBashWithOperations` does, so the call-site marker and the oracle's `droppedFromWindow` allowance are gone and `TestExecuteBashWithOperationsMatchesPi` compares the results as they are. `TestExecuteBashWithOperationsReportsDiscardedChunks10164` and `TestBashExecutionToTextNamesTheLogOfDiscardedOutput` hold the behavior.

- D245: The last-seen changelog version is read as its leading major.minor.patch; an unreadable one shows nothing and records the current version. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 9a1953961 (fix(coding-agent): ignore prerelease suffix of last seen changelog version, #11753): the pinned Pi's `getNewEntries` reads the leading `major.minor.patch` of the recorded version and returns undefined for an unreadable one, as `GetNewEntries` does, so the call-site markers and the oracle tests' `pinnedReadsLastSeenLikeD245` skip are gone. `TestGetNewEntriesIgnoresPrereleaseAndBuildSuffixes`, `TestRecordChangelogVersionRecordsCurrentVersionForUnreadableLastSeen`, `TestExtensionChangelogUpdatesUpstream`, `TestChangelogMatchesPiOracle` and the random changelog oracle hold the behavior.

- D247: Quit settles the interrupted run before session_shutdown and disposal. Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main 7a53c310a (`agent-session-runtime.ts` `dispose`, #9126) awaits `session.abort()` before it emits `session_shutdown`. `TestRuntimeCloseDuringToolSettlesTheInterruptedTurnBeforeShutdown` ports Pi's regression test. The ID remains reserved.

- D248: The /tree search line draws a cursor while the selector is focused. Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main 2cfab7c26 (`tree-selector.ts` `SearchLine`, #9157) ends the line with `CURSOR_MARKER` and a reverse-video cell while the selector is focused and no label is edited, and draws a trailing space for an empty query. `TestTreeSelectSearchCursor` ports Pi's three tests, the tree-selector oracle tests compare the line byte for byte, and the tmux scenarios `tree/06-tree-header-spacing` and `interactive-rendering/65-app-action-keys` compare the cursor cell too. The ID remains reserved.

- D249: Images in queued steering and follow-up messages are resized when the message is delivered. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 4cf69332c (fix(coding-agent): resize images in queued steering and follow-up messages, #11782): the pinned Pi marks a queued message that carries images and normalizes them with `_normalizePromptImages` at delivery, as `Session.QueuedUserMessage` and `normalizeDeliveredImages` do, so the call-site markers are gone. PiG keeps the marks in `Session.queuedImageMessages`, cleared by `Session.ClearQueue` and delivery, where Pi's `WeakSet` also releases them on garbage collection. `TestInteractiveQueuedMessagesAreBuiltBySessionForImageNormalization`, `TestSessionQueuedUserMessageBuiltForAgentQueueIsResizedAtDelivery` and `TestSessionResizesImagesInQueuedSteeringAndFollowUpMessages` hold the behavior.

- D250: A delivered image-only queued message leaves the pending queue. Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main a63c4ec5c (`fix(coding-agent): clear delivered image-only queue entries`, #8612, fixing #8581) is in the pin c23b9ec1, so `agent-session.ts` `_handleAgentEvent` matches an empty delivered text against the pending queues and the RPC stream carries the resulting `queue_update` before the user `message_start`. `TestSessionRemovesAnImageOnlySteeringMessageAfterDelivery` ports Pi's test, `TestSessionEmptyQueuedMessagesPublishTheQueueTheyLeaveAtDelivery` names the records, and the RPC scenarios `20-rpc-runtime-boundary-values` and `35-rpc-queue-prompt-await` compare them with the pin's Pi and pin both records with `both_contain`. The ID remains reserved.

- D251: A draft moved out of and back into the editor keeps its collapsed paste content. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 51313540a (fix(coding-agent): preserve pasted text during editor restoration, #10146): the pinned Pi reads `getExpandedText()` where it moves the editor draft elsewhere and back (`setCustomEditorComponent`, `showExtensionCustom`, `restoreQueuedMessagesToEditor`), as the four call sites do, so the markers are gone. `TestRemoteOverlayEditorSlotKeepsCollapsedPasteContent`, `TestExtUIContextCustomKeepsCollapsedPasteContent`, `TestRestoreQueuedMessagesKeepsCollapsedPasteContent` and `TestSetRemoteEditorKeepsCollapsedPasteContent` hold the behavior.

- D252: Truncating text never splits a surrogate pair. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 9e870f6f0 (fix(coding-agent): do not split surrogate pairs when truncating text, #11757): the pinned Pi cuts by UTF-16 units with `sliceHead` and `sliceTail`, which keep a pair whole, at the grep line, codemode, compaction summary and MCP truncation sites, as `jsstring.SliceHead`, `SliceTail`, `HeadUnits` and `TailUnits` do, so the call-site markers, the Node reference adjustment of the SDK conformance suite and the oracle script change are gone. `TestSliceHeadAndTailNeverSplitAPair`, `TestSliceHeadAndTailMatchSliceWithoutSurrogates`, `TestTruncateLine`, `TestTruncateForSummaryDropsASplitSurrogatePair`, `TestTruncateOutputKeepsSurrogatePairsWhole`, `TestTruncateDropsASplitSurrogatePair`, `TestStderrTailDropsASplitSurrogatePair`, the SDK truncation tests and `TestConformance_TruncationHelpersAgreeAcrossSDKs` hold the behavior.

- D253: find and ls read a limit below 1 as 1, and find reports the limit only when more results exist. Retired by the upstream pin: Pi main 676af1d77 (`fix(coding-agent): find and ls limit edge cases`, #11747, fixing #7121) is in the pin c23b9ec1, so both tools use `max(1, floor(limit ?? default))`, find asks for `limit + 1` results, and a custom glob receives `limit + 1`. `TestFindLimitEdgeCases7121` and `TestLsLimitEdgeCases7121` port Pi's cases, and the scenarios `tools/21-find-operations-and-limits` and `tools/15-builtin-tool-wire-details` compare the limit lines with the pin's Pi byte for byte. The ID remains reserved.

- D254: `pig config` loads the user's keybindings. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi b0c64f33c (fix(coding-agent): load user keybindings in pi config, #11788): the pinned Pi's `selectConfig()` installs `KeybindingsManager.create(agentDir)`, so the remaps of `keybindings.json` work in the config selector, as `newConfigSelectorUI` does, so the call-site marker is gone. `TestConfigSelectorUIUsesTheUsersKeybindings` holds the behavior.

- D255: `pig mcp list` sends the provider's token. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 4d72d05bc (fix(coding-agent): send provider tokens in pi mcp list): the pinned Pi's `pi mcp list` sends the token of the provider named by `auth.provider`, reports `sign in with: /login <provider> in pi` and refuses `mcp login` and `mcp logout` for such a server, as `RunMcpCommand` does, so the three call-site markers are gone. `TestMcpCommandSendsTheTokenOfTheProviderNamedByAuthProvider` and `TestMcpProviderTokenReaderReadsTheAgentDirectoryCredentials` hold the behavior.

- D256: An unreachable llama.cpp server error names the server and the cause. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi ea0f49a36 (fix(coding-agent): name the llama.cpp server and cause when it cannot be reached, #11779): the pinned Pi's `fetchServer` reads `Could not reach llama.cpp server at <url>: <cause>` for a network-level failure, as `unreachableError` does (the cause is Go's own transport text where Node's is `connect ECONNREFUSED`), so the call-site markers are gone. `TestClientRequestErrorsMirrorFetch`, `TestModelsProviderRefreshReportsNetworkErrorsAndAbortedCallers` and `TestLlamaCommandOffersRetryWhenTheServerIsUnreachable` hold the behavior.

- D257: The extension selector draws a window of at most ten options with a position indicator. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 3b57ed7e2 (fix: limit selector window height, #11072): the pinned Pi's `ExtensionSelectorComponent` draws a window of up to 10 options centered on the cursor with a `(selection/total)` line, as `visibleWindow` and `selectorMaxVisible` do, so the call-site markers are gone. `TestExtensionSelectorWindowsLongOptionListsAroundTheSelection`, `TestExtensionSelectorShortAndEmptyListsHaveNoScrollIndicator` and `TestExtensionSelectLongListShowsAWindowAroundTheCursor` hold the behavior.

- D258: Model cycling selects the only model in scope and cycles into the scope from outside it. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 5732bac06 (fix(coding-agent): cycle to the only model in scope when the active model is outside it, #11832): the pinned Pi's `_cycleScopedModel` and `_cycleAvailableModel` select the first entry forward and the last backward when the active model is outside the list, and the only model of a one-entry list, as `nextCycleIndex` does, so the call-site marker and the replay stop of `TestAgentSessionMatchesPi` (`cycleDiffersFromPinnedPi`) are gone and all 600 seeded scenarios compare in full. `TestSessionModelExtensionCyclesUpstream`, `TestSessionCycleModelSeesExtensionProviderRegistration`, `TestSessionModelCycleBoundaries` and `TestAgentSessionMatchesPi` hold the behavior.

- D259: Alacritty is detected for OSC 8 hyperlinks without TERM_PROGRAM. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 2af8a42f5 (fix(tui): detect Alacritty for OSC 8 hyperlinks without TERM_PROGRAM, #11777): the pinned Pi's `detectCapabilitiesFromEnvironment` also treats `TERM` beginning with `alacritty` and a non-empty `ALACRITTY_WINDOW_ID` as Alacritty, as `tui.DetectCapabilities` does, so the call-site marker is gone. `TestUpstreamTerminalImageCapabilities` (the Alacritty by TERM and by ALACRITTY_WINDOW_ID cases) holds the behavior.

- D260: .dart, .nix, .groovy and .gradle files are highlighted in read and write views. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi c13f6acfb (Highlight .dart, .nix, and .groovy files in read/write views, #11790): the pinned Pi's `getLanguageFromPath` maps `dart`, `nix`, `groovy` and `gradle` (as `groovy`), as `tui.LanguageFromPath` and the Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs' `GetLanguageFromPath` do, so the call-site marker and the oracle's `d260Extension` skip are gone. `TestLanguageFromPath`, `TestLanguageFromPathMatchesPi`, `TestHighlightCodeHighlightsDartNixAndGroovy` and `TestConformance_LanguageFromPathAgreesAcrossSDKs` hold the behavior.

- D261: SSE lines end at CR, LF or CRLF. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi b64e6badd (fix(mcp): recognize CR-only line endings in Streamable HTTP SSE, #11775): the pinned Pi's `consumeSseStream` ends a line at CR, LF or CRLF and delivers a CR-terminated event while the stream is open, as `sseLineSplitter` does, so the call-site marker is gone. `TestConsumeSSEStreamRecognizesCRLFAndCRLineEndingsIncludingCRLFSplitAcrossChunks` and `TestConsumeSSEStreamDispatchesCRTerminatedEventsWhileTheStreamStaysOpen` hold the behavior.

- D262: The startup benchmark disposes the session runtime. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi efb45b51d (fix(coding-agent): dispose session runtime after startup benchmark, #11736): the pinned Pi disposes the session runtime after `interactiveMode.stop()` in the startup benchmark path and before the timings print, as `runStartupBenchmark` does (PiG also disposes it when initialization or stop fails), so the call-site marker is gone. `TestStartupBenchmarkDisposesTheRuntimeAfterStoppingInteractiveMode` holds the behavior.

- D310: `/reload` and a session switch remove every extension overlay. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi 80d4d3c86 (fix(coding-agent): remove all extension overlays on /reload and session switch, #11853): the pinned Pi's `resetExtensionUI` removes every extension overlay, hidden ones included, and keeps pi's own, as `removeExtensionOverlays` does, so the two call-site markers are gone. `TestResetRemovesEveryExtensionOverlayButKeepsPisOwn` holds the behavior.

- D311: `ctrl+o` applies to user bash output shown while the agent is working. Retired by the pin `1.5.1`, which contains Pi acd059b72 (fix(coding-agent): apply ctrl+o to user bash output shown while the agent is working, #11886): the pinned Pi starts the output of `!` and `!!` commands run while the agent works in the current expand state, keeps it in the pending-message area and moves it into the chat when the run settles, as `handleBashCommand`, `updatePendingMessagesDisplay` and `flushPendingBashBlocks` do, so the call-site markers are gone. `TestCtrlOExpandsUserBashOutputInThePendingArea`, `TestQueuedMessageUpdatesKeepUserBashOutputInThePendingArea`, `TestNewUserBashOutputStartsInTheCurrentExpandState`, `TestSettlingTheRunMovesUserBashOutputToTheChat` and `TestInteractiveBashDuringRunStaysPendingUntilTheRunSettles` hold the behavior.

## Active divergences

D78, D82 and D83 record owner-approved known gaps for 0.3.x (decision 2026-09-28). Approval records a difference; it does not prove parity, waive an unrelated defect, or turn a failing comparison into a pass. Same-process object behavior must remain Pi-exact. See `docs/findings/0.3.0-known-gaps.md` for the integration boundary and retained failures.

## D2 PiG uses a separate command and configuration identity

What: the command, startup banner, and terminal title use `pig`. Configuration defaults to `~/.pig` and project `.pig`. A binary resolved under the name `pi` exits with status 2 instead of shadowing Pi.

The exact environment value `PIG_USE_PI_DIRS=1` explicitly selects Pi's agent and project directories. Shared mode uses `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` or `~/.pi/agent`, project `<cwd>/.pi`, and `PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR` rather than the two `PIG_CODING_AGENT_*` overrides. `--session-dir` and the `sessionDir` setting keep Pi's precedence. Project trust, Package/resource discovery, system prompt files, sessions, and imported Node configuration helpers all use the selected directories. PiG-owned SDK caches, docs and user Piglet state keep the PiG configuration root. No files are copied, no trees are merged, and no setting inside a relocated directory controls the selection.

Owner decision: 2026-09-27, release 0.3.0. Pi's config.ts:528-573 selects its directory identity before loading settings; a process-level opt-in avoids circular settings resolution. `PIG_CODING_AGENT_DIR=~/.pi/agent` alone remains an agent-only relocation and does not select project `.pi` or configure external adapters.

Shared auth/settings/trust and dynamic catalog cache writes use Pi's lock-directory protocol. Auth JSON preserves provider fields and required empty OAuth fields; existing auth modes and ACLs remain intact. PiG reads but does not write models.json. Session JSONL is interoperable, but simultaneous edits of one session are not supported by either host. pi-acp owns `~/.pi/pi-acp/session-map.json`; PiG does not rewrite it. Use one adapter process per home and Pi-compatible resources in shared settings. This opt-in does not make Piglets or native PiG extensions loadable by Pi.

Evidence: `TestPiDirectoriesOptIn`, `TestPiDirectoriesProjectSettingsAndSessions`, `TestPiDirectoriesSessionOverridePrecedence`, `TestPiSharedFilesRoundTrip`, `TestPiSharedFilesConcurrentWriters`, and `settings/10-pi-directories-opt-in`. The real Powerline and pi-acp replay and file-lock limits are recorded in `docs/findings/pi-directories-opt-in.md`.

Why: separate command and configuration identities prevent accidental writes to
Pi state.

Remove when: never.

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/guard.go`, `coding/cli/main.go`, `internal/codingagent/paths.go`, `internal/codingagent/startup_header.go`, `internal/codingagent/ext_ui_context.go`, `coding/piglogin/doc.go`, `coding/piglogin/registry.go`, `coding/extension/host/subprocess/ui_bridge.go`, `coding/extension/builtin/builtin.go`, `coding/cli/project_trust.go`, `coding/cli/extension_set.go`, and `internal/codingagent/project_trust_warning.go`, `internal/evals/harness.go`.

Every message that tells the user to run a command names `pig`, never `pi`: the replaced built-in extension warning says `pig config` where Pi's resource-loader.ts says `pi config`, and the startup hint says `pig -ne`. `TestProductionStringsNeverInstructAPiCommand` rejects a production string literal that tells the user to run `pi`.

The trust prompt and warning name the selected project directory and the `pig` command. Their wording, styling, and conditions otherwise match Pi. `test/parity/scenarios/project-trust/09-cancel-trust-shows-warning-with-extensions-off.toml` and `10-startup-trust-prompt-wording.toml` compare these surfaces with only the D2 identity substitution.

The startup header shows PiG's pig head in Pi's logo slot, 7 rows instead of 2, so PiG never presents itself as Pi; the hints, help and resource listing otherwise match Pi. Owner approved 2026-10-01; after trying a smaller 12-by-8 head live, the owner chose the sprite's 16-by-14 pig at its original size, with the version and hint lines beside it. The version beside the head, and in the help and diagnostics banner, is PiG's composite version: the PiG release with the Pi release it ports as semver build metadata (for example `0.7.0+1.5.0`). `pig --version` is not that: it prints Pi's bare version exactly as `pi --version` does, and `pig version` names the PiG release and the Pi pin in separate fields.

The head is the sprite's 16-by-14 pig, drawn in half blocks as 16 cells by 7 lines: the standard pig recolored for the color sprites, and each character's original art. `TestEveryHeadIsTheSharedGrid` and `TestEveryHeadIsTheSpritesPig` hold each one to the shared grid and the sprite's own art. Pi prefixes its 2-line logo to the first two lines of the header text and renders all of it as one Text (interactive-mode.ts:998-1006 in the v1.0.0 upstream mirror); PiG prefixes the head's 7 lines to the first seven lines in the same way: the version beside the first, then in compact mode the first hint line, the `Press` line, the blank line and the onboarding line, or in expanded mode the first six hints. A line beside the head wraps in the cells right of the head; the rest wraps at Pi's full width. The compact header is therefore 2 lines taller than Pi's, the expanded header has Pi's 22 lines, and a line beside the head wraps in `width-19` cells where Pi's wraps in `width-7`.

The head is drawn only when it fits with the version beside it (31 columns with a 12-character version), the terminal aligns half blocks, and the theme's color mode is truecolor. Otherwise, in 256-color terminals and narrow panes, the one-line text mark `PiG.` takes Pi's logo slot: it is 4 cells wide like Pi's logo, its line carries the version and the slot's second line the first line of key hints (interactive-mode.ts:1002-1006), so the hints wrap where Pi's wrap. In Apple Terminal, where Pi draws its text wordmark and the hints below it (`piWordmark`), PiG draws the same layout with the text mark. "PiG" is bold, and as Pi colors its "Pi" wordmark, each letter takes a mid or dark stop of the sprite's wordmark ramp (rows 8 to 10, at least 3:1 against both white and black, falling back to the period color where a ramp is too pale, as the default sprite's is); the period is in the sprite's wordmark period color. The 256- and 16-color fallbacks apply, as in Apple Terminal. Only the composite version and the onboarding product name otherwise use PiG's identity.

The built-in header is the one `setHeader(undefined)` and `/reload` restore, so Pi's logo never appears. An extension replaces it with `ctx.ui.setHeader` as in Pi, and an extension or Piglet replaces it with its own native login through `ctx.ui.setLogin` (D60). The built-in `pig-login` extension (`coding/piglogin`) registers `/sprite`, which chooses one of fifteen built-in sprites (`pig-default`, then the colors `pink`, `green`, `mint`, `sandy`, `grey`, `blush`, `lavender`, `cloud`, then the characters `pigrogu`, `darth-vader`, `kratos`, `piglet`, `spider-ham`, `sheriff`) and saves the choice in `$PIG_HOME/state/pig-standard/login.json`. `/sprite preview [id]` shows a sprite's full art, its `PiG.` wordmark and pig as the native login template draws them, with its name and tagline, in an overlay that any key closes. The picker `/sprite` opens draws the pig of the highlighted sprite right after its option rows, a few cells past the longest row and top-aligned with the first option (dropped on narrow terminals), so the choice shows its face before it is made; `Create your own...` is not a sprite and draws no pig, and the selector keeps its full-width rows there. A UI context that cannot mount a custom component (the RPC and no-op contexts) gets the same rows from the plain extension selector with no preview column. Pi has no `/sprite`. Clicking the head, or the text mark where it stands in for Pi's logo, plays PiG's version of Pi's logo animation (D87).

An extension or Piglet adds sprites with `ctx.ui.registerSprite` (wire call `ui.registerSprite`; Go `RegisterSprite`, Rust `register_sprite`, Python `register_sprite`, Node and TypeScript `registerSprite`). A `SpriteDefinition` has a lowercase slug `id` of at most 32 characters, a one-line `name` and `tagline`, a 16-by-14 `mascot` of palette symbols with `.` transparent, which the header draws as the head and the preview beside the wordmark, and a `palette` of `#RRGGBB` colors; unknown fields fail. The host validates the definition (`invalid_sprite`), rejects a built-in id or another extension's id (`ui_error`), and replaces the sprite when the same extension registers the id again. Registered sprites follow the built-in ones in `/sprite list` and the picker and are saved like them. The host replays them to each new UI and drops them when their extension goes away. While the saved sprite is not registered, the header draws `pig-default` and the saved choice stays. Pi has no sprites, so this is part of the same identity exception, approved with it (owner, 2026-10-01).

Locked by: `test/parity/scenarios/startup/00-startup-banner.toml` (the head's lines with the version, the first hints and the `Press` line), `02-startup-compact-help.toml`, `03-startup-expanded-help.toml`, `04-startup-verbose-collapse.toml` and `14-startup-quiet-header.toml` (every hint byte for byte with Pi, with the head's 16 cells removed), `13-startup-narrow-mark-wrapping.toml` (26 columns, the text mark, byte-equal with Pi from the first hint on), `coding/piglogin` (the head and art goldens, the text mark, the registry, `/sprite list`, `set`, `preview`, the picker's rows, right-side pig, cursor follow, previewless create row and plain-selector fallback), `internal/codingagent/pi_logo_upstream_test.go` (the head against the goldens, the layout, the head threshold, the text mark in narrow, 256-color and Apple terminals, replacement by `setHeader` and by a Piglet's `setLogin`, `/reload`, the picker's pig and its Escape through the interactive host), `coding/extension/host/subprocess/ui_bridge_sprite_test.go`, the `sprite-probe` row of `test/extension-conformance`, `coding/cli/extension_sprite_pty_test.go` (a Go, Python and Rust extension's sprite through list, set, preview and restarts with and without the extension, and a click on the head in the default fullscreen mode, in the real binary),
`tui/terminal_test.go` (`TestBuildTerminalTitle_NoName`), and the command
identity tests in `cmd/pig`.
SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D14 tools-manager archive extraction uses Go stdlib instead of spawning tar/unzip/PowerShell

Upstream `tools-manager.ts` (`extractTarGzArchive`, `extractZipArchive`)
spawns `tar`, `unzip`, and (on Windows) PowerShell `Expand-Archive` to
unpack downloaded fd/rg release assets. Each platform has a fallback chain:
Linux/macOS try `unzip` then `tar`; Windows tries `tar.exe` (bsdtar) then
PowerShell.

pig `internal/codingagent/tools/tools_manager.go` uses Go stdlib
(`archive/tar` + `compress/gzip` + `archive/zip`) on all platforms. The
extracted binary is byte-identical for every fd/rg release asset published
to date; only two observable differences exist:

1. Error message text on extraction failure. Upstream produces e.g.
   `Failed to extract X: unzip: ...; tar: ...`. pig produces e.g.
   `Failed to extract X: <go-error>`. There is no parity scenario for the
   failure path (it requires a malformed archive on the GitHub CDN).

2. Symlink and hardlink handling. Upstream `tar xzf` preserves them on
   Unix. pig silently skips `TypeSymlink`/`TypeLink` entries. fd and rg
   archives contain manpage symlinks (`man/man1/fd.1` → `fd.1`) that
   neither binary actually needs; pig's behavior is therefore safe in
   practice but theoretically observable if a user `ls`-es the install
   dir.

Why this is a divergence (not a bug): the Go stdlib path is more reliable
than spawning system tools: it doesn't depend on `unzip` being installed
(Alpine, minimal Docker, Termux), can't be tricked by a hostile `tar` on
PATH, and produces deterministic error text regardless of locale. The
divergence is structural to the Go port: faithfully calling `os/exec.Command("unzip", ...)` would re-introduce the very brittleness Go's stdlib lets us
avoid.

Remove when: upstream uses an equivalent in-process archive reader, or PiG uses
the same external extraction contract.

Locked by: unit tests in `internal/codingagent/tools/tools_manager_test.go`
(TestDownloadTool_TarGz_NestedBinary, TestDownloadTool_TarGz_FlatBinary,
TestDownloadTool_Zip, TestDownloadTool_DeeplyNestedBinary,
TestExtractZip_PathTraversalRejected, TestExtractTarGz_PathTraversalRejected)
that exercise the full extract pipeline against synthetic fd/rg archives.

PORT_MAP path: `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts`
SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D26 PiG never identifies itself as Pi to a service

What: every string upstream sends to identify Pi, and every Pi-owned endpoint it calls, is PiG's own. One file, `internal/coding/pigidentity/identity.json`, holds the values. The Go host reads it (`internal/coding/pigidentity`), and `automation/gen/pi-identity-patches.mjs` applies the same values to the Pi JavaScript the Node extension runtime vendors, so a Node extension that calls the Pi SDK (`createAgentSession`) or pi-ai sends the identity the Go host sends. The vendoring step (`automation/gen/vendor-pi-dist.sh`) runs the patch before it compiles the SDK bundle, so a re-vendor keeps it, and `vendor-manifest.json` labels each patched file. An exact-line patch that no longer matches a new Pi release fails the vendoring step.

Replaced values (audited against upstream 0.87.1; `pi-identity-patches.mjs` applies them to the vendored 0.99.2 dist and fails when a literal is missing):
- Provider attribution (`PI_ATTRIBUTION` in `packages/ai/src/utils/identity.ts`; the agent loop, the durable Harness and the coding agent send it, the coding agent only while install telemetry is enabled, unchanged gate): `{ name: "pi", url: "https://pi.dev", categories: ["cli-agent"] }` → `{ name: "PiG", url: "https://github.com/MichaelKinsy/PiG", categories: ["cli-agent"] }`, which `getAttributionHeaders` maps to OpenRouter `HTTP-Referer`, `X-OpenRouter-Title` and `X-OpenRouter-Categories`, NVIDIA NIM `X-BILLING-INVOKE-ORIGIN: PiG` and OpenCode `x-opencode-client: PiG` (it was `pig` before the name became one field). Pi's Cloudflare `User-Agent: pi-coding-agent` attribution no longer exists upstream and is gone. The OpenCode `x-opencode-session` header is not attribution and stays independent of the telemetry gate. Host and provider matching are unchanged. Node: `utils/identity.js` in the vendored pi-ai, which `createAgentSession` reaches through the coding agent's `getClientIdentity`.
- User agents: D65 (`pig/<coding.Version> (<platform> <release>; <arch>)`) now also covers the vendored `getPiUserAgent` of coding-agent and pi-ai. The host names its composite version to each Node runtime process in `PIG_PRODUCT_VERSION`.
- OpenAI Codex: `originator: pi` on the SSE request, the websocket handshake and the OAuth authorize URL → `pig`. xAI device-code `referrer: pi` → `pig`.
- Child processes: `AI_AGENT=pi` → `AI_AGENT=pig` (Pi's `cli/setup.ts` and `rpc-entry.ts` export it so tools name the launching agent). `PI_CODING_AGENT=true` is unchanged, because Pi extensions read it to detect the harness.
- Pi-owned endpoints the vendored Node code would call, routed as the Go host does (D62, D64): the version check (`utils/version-check.js`), the remote model catalog overlay (`core/remote-catalog-provider.js`), the install report (`interactive-mode.js`) and the managed installer API (`package-manager-cli.js`) go to `https://pi-in-go.dev`. The bug-report upload (`core/bug-report-upload.js`) refuses, because PiG never uploads bug reports (D62). The Radius share upload (`session-share.js` `tryShareViaRadius`) returns "unavailable", because PiG never sends a Radius token to Radius's share gateway (D64). None of these run in a PiG session today (the Go host implements the interactive commands), so the patches close paths that an extension could reach by importing Pi's `InteractiveMode`, `getLatestPiRelease`, `withRemoteCatalog` or `uploadBugReport`.

Kept as Pi sends them, by design: GitHub Copilot's `GitHubCopilotChat/0.35.0`, `Editor-Version`, `Editor-Plugin-Version` and `Copilot-Integration-Id` headers and Anthropic OAuth's `claude-cli/…` user agent, because those providers accept requests only from the client they name; Radius's `radius.pi.dev` gateway, model catalog and `pi-gateway` OAuth client id, which the user selects by logging in to the `radius` provider (Pi's own product, the same in the Go host); Pi documentation and migration-guide links printed as text and the update notification's changelog link; `pi-messages` (a protocol name). The guard test `TestVendoredRuntimeCarriesNoPiIdentityOutsideTheAllowlist` scans every vendored file and lists each remaining literal with its reason.

Why: every upstream identity value is pi-branded (`pi.dev`, `pi`, `Pi`, `pi-coding-agent`). A rebranded port must not misattribute its traffic to Pi, so PiG substitutes its own branding host-for-host and header-for-header instead of narrowing upstream's provider set, and sends nothing to a service PiG does not operate on the user's behalf. The Node extension runtime ships Pi's own JavaScript, which is a second way out of the process; the owner directive for 0.3.1 is that no path identify as Pi. Only the literal values and the disabled Pi-only uploads change; the provider and host matching, the telemetry gate and the OpenCode session pair are unchanged from upstream.

`IsInstallTelemetryEnabled` mirrors upstream `isInstallTelemetryEnabled` (telemetry.ts:8): `PI_TELEMETRY` env truthiness wins when set, otherwise the `enableInstallTelemetry` setting (default true). This is the same gate that also covers the install/update ping, sent to PiG's own `pi-in-go.dev` endpoint instead of pi.dev (D64; `internal/codingagent/install_telemetry.go`).

Unverified against the live services: OpenAI's Codex backend and login and xAI's device-code endpoint accept an `originator`/`referrer` other than `pi` (Pi's own client and other Codex clients use their own names). No credentials were available to run them.

Remove when: PiG sends byte-identical (unbranded) identity to services, which would require PiG to claim it is Pi. Not planned.

- `ai/openai_responses.go`, `ai/openai_codex_responses.go`, `ai/openai_codex_websocket.go`, `ai/oauth_openai_codex.go`, `ai/oauth_xai.go`: the Codex originator and the xAI referrer. `ai/user_agent.go`: the product name in D65's user agent.
- `coding/cli/setup_cli.go`: `AI_AGENT`.
- `coding/extension/host/subprocess/terminal_capabilities_env.go`: `PIG_PRODUCT_VERSION` for the Node runtime.
- `automation/gen/pi-identity-patches.mjs` and `coding/extension/host/subprocess/runtime-node/shims/pig-identity.mjs`: the Node runtime patches and the user-agent builder they call.
- `internal/codingagent/settings.go`: `IsInstallTelemetryEnabled`, `isTruthyTelemetryEnvFlag`.

Locked by: `internal/coding/pigidentity` `TestIdentityValuesAreThePiGBrand`, `TestIdentityJSONIsTheSingleSource`; `coding` `TestModelRuntimeRequestsCarryPiGAttributionNeverPis` (Go host, real HTTP), `TestMergeProviderAttributionHeadersMatchesPinnedProvidersAndHosts`, `TestProviderAttributionWrapperReachesHTTPRequest`, `TestBuildModelGatesAttributionHeadersOnInstallTelemetrySetting`; `cmd/pig` `TestNodeExtensionModelCallsCarryPiGIdentityNeverPis` (the real binary, a Node extension's `createAgentSession` and pi-ai `completeSimple` against OpenRouter-, NVIDIA- and OpenCode-shaped servers) and `TestSetupCliSetsInheritedProcessMarkers`; `test/extension-conformance` `TestSDKModelCallsCarryPiGIdentityNeverPis` (the Go, Python and Rust SDKs' host model calls); `coding/extension/host/subprocess` `TestVendoredRuntimeCarriesNoPiIdentityOutsideTheAllowlist`, `TestVendoredRuntimeRequestsAreNeverMadeAsPi`, `TestVendoredPiDistMatchesThePinnedPackage` (the patch table), `TestNodeExtensionUserAgentNamesPiGAndItsVersion`; `ai` `TestLoginOpenAICodexAuthorizeURLNamesPiGAsOriginator`, `TestCodexWebSocketHeadersNamePiGAsOriginator` and the Codex and xAI request tests; `internal/codingagent` `TestHostedEndpointsUseTheIdentityOrigin`, `TestSettingsManager_IsInstallTelemetryEnabled`; `ai` `TestProviderIdentityHeadersMatchPi` and `TestOpenRouterImagesHeadersMatchPi` (the pinned Pi and PiG map the same identity, Pi's own `{ name: "pi", ... }` included, to the same OpenRouter, NVIDIA and OpenCode headers, so PiG's `PiG` values differ only through `PiAttribution`).
