The install is the one place where PiG waits as Pi's `close` does, so it follows the exit instead.

Pi bug: Pi's `installDependencies` hangs until a process that inherited npm's pipes ends (`child.on("close", resolve)`, `install.ts:42`). Not reported upstream.

Scope: `InstallDependencies` only, in the case a process other than the command holds the command's stdout or stderr after the command exited. `WaitDelay` also counts from an abort: when the context ends, the command is sent SIGTERM as before, and a command that ignores it is now killed five seconds later and the install returns the abort's reason then (measured 6.0 s, against 30.1 s on 989927eff4, where `Run` waited for the command to end by itself). Pi rejects at once and sends SIGTERM and does not kill, so the abort path is closer to Pi, not further. Nothing differs when the command's streams close with it (every case Pi's tests cover): arguments, working directory, output text on failure, the abort path and the exit-code messages are Pi's.

Owner decision: 2026-11-05, approved through the coordinator for 0.7.1.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi stops waiting on inherited pipes (its install settles on the command's `exit`, or bounds the wait for `close`). Then the call-site marker and this record go.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_install.go` (`InstallDependencies`, the `WaitDelay` and the `ErrWaitDelay` case).

Evidence: `TestInstallDependenciesEndsWithTheCommandThoughAProcessItStartedHoldsItsPipes` (`chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_install_test.go`; red on 989927eff4: the install did not return in 25 s for exit 0 and for exit 1).

Parity allowance: none needed; Pi's install tests (`install.test.ts`, ported in `plugins_install_test.go`) pass unchanged, and none of them leaves a process holding the pipes.

## D268 The extension SDKs read tool arguments of any depth the host can send; Pi's runtime fails at a depth set by V8's stack

What: Pi's own runtime (the Node SDK) returns a tool's arguments only while V8 has stack for `JSON.stringify`. On Node 24.22.1 with the default stack, arguments shaped `{"zeta":1,"meta":{"d":[...]}}` come back as an object at d=4300 and fail with `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` at d=4400 (`JSON.stringify` fails past 4459 at the default stack and past 9103 with `--stack-size=2000`; plain `JSON.parse` reads any depth). The limit is V8 stack exhaustion: it moves with the call depth, the Node version, the platform and `--stack-size`, so no fixed Pi depth exists to reproduce. PiG's Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs return the object, and the texts Pi writes for it, for every frame the host can send. An argument value can nest 10000 levels (the host's marshal validates it alone against 10000: d=9998 in the row shape above). None of the four ends its extension or its connection on deeper input: a request is answered with the RangeError message, a host call result is an error, a notification is dropped with a line on stderr. The Node SDK is Pi's own runtime; PiG's Node runtime copies each event-stream payload for its handler (iteratively), and a handler receives the object as in Pi, up to where V8's own `JSON.stringify` runs out of stack (about 4400 levels on Node 24).

| SDK | What reads the frame | Bound |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Go | `json.UnmarshalFrame` (`extensions/sdk/json`; `Unmarshal` and the host's marshal keep the standard 10000) | 10064 levels of the whole frame |
| Python | `json.loads`, then a non-recursive parser (`_loads_deep`); ordering and stringify are iterative | 10100 levels (`MAX_FRAME_DEPTH`); `json.loads` alone stops at about 9997 on 3.12 and about 990 on 3.11 |
| Rust | `from_json_str` on a 64 MiB decode stack (the arguments of a request or notification and the operations of an event-stream delta); a request with such a value runs on a 64 MiB thread, the connection's main loop on an 8 MiB one | 10016 levels (`MAX_NESTING_DEPTH`) |
| Lisp | the JSON reader and writer recurse to 256 levels and keep deeper containers on a stack of their own; ordering, event-stream copies, `JSON.stringify` of conversation values, the bigint check and palette comparison are iterative (the image keeps SBCL's default 2 MB control stack) | 10016 levels (`*max-json-depth*`) |
| Node | Pi's own `JSON.parse` and `JSON.stringify` | V8's stack (about 4400 levels on Node 24) |

Why: Before, an extension could die on arguments Pi's own host sends it. The Go SDK ended its connection from d=9997: its reader validated the whole frame against encoding/json's 10000-level limit, while the host validates a request's arguments alone, so a frame the host sends nests a few levels past it (a limit of the codec, not a stack). The Python SDK failed the call from about 1000 levels, and from about 990 on Python 3.11, because its ordering and `JSON.stringify` were recursive and its JSON reader is bound by the recursion limit. The Rust and Lisp readers ended the connection past 4096 and 2000 levels. Rust's `Context::tool_params` returned none past 128 levels. Rust's event-stream deltas (a `message_update` whose tool call carries deep arguments, a `tool_execution_update`) ended the connection past 128 levels. Matching Pi's observable result for the arguments Pi accepts, and failing a call (never the extension) beyond what an SDK reads, keeps tool calls with legitimately nested JSON working. Pi's exact cut-off is not reproducible.

Pi bug: none; Pi's limit is the stack of the JavaScript engine, not a defect to report.

Scope: arguments, results and events (the whole payload of an event and the operations of a `message_update` or `tool_execution_update` delta) read by the Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs, in every placement (isolated and packed), up to the host's 10000 levels. Not the Node SDK. Not a value nested past 10000 levels (the host refuses to send one).

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi parses and returns tool arguments without a stack-bound depth limit, or the owner decides the SDKs should stop at Pi's observed depth. Then the call-site markers and this record go.

Call-site markers: `extensions/sdk/protocol.go` (`readLoop`), `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/__init__.py` (`Extension._read`), `extensions/sdk-rs/src/protocol.rs` (`MAX_NESTING_DEPTH`), `extensions/sdk-lisp/src/json.lisp` (`*max-json-depth*`; Lisp is not scanned by `check-divergence-consistency.sh`).

Evidence: `TestToolParamsNestingAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/tool_params_deep_test.go`; red on 03c551f3e2 for Go at d=9998, and for Python, Rust and Lisp), `TestModelStreamEventWithDeepToolArgumentsReachesEverySDK` (`model_stream_deep_event_test.go`), `TestDeepEventStreamDeltasReachEverySDK` (`deep_event_delta_test.go`; deep `message_update` and `tool_execution_update` deltas, red for Rust on c1da695c1d), both over isolated and packed placements, and each SDK's own suite for the frames past its bound (`extensions/sdk/protocol_deep_frame_test.go`, `extensions/sdk-py/tests/test_deep_frames.py`, `extensions/sdk-rs/src/surrogate_tests.rs`, `extensions/sdk-lisp/tests/json-tests.lisp`).

Parity allowance: the row pins every SDK's outcome at depths 200, 1000, 4000, 5000, 9000 and 9998: the object for the Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs at all six; for the Node SDK (Pi's own runtime, pinned Node 24.22.1) the object at 200, 1000 and 4000 and V8's RangeError at 5000, 9000 and 9998. The event rows (`TestDeepEventStreamDeltasReachEverySDK`) pin the payload of a `message_update` or `tool_execution_update` delta, in arrays and in objects, at 200, 3000, 4000, 9000 (and 1 after them): every SDK hands its handler the payload, except that the Node SDK's handler at 9000 throws V8's RangeError from its own `JSON.stringify`, as a handler does in Pi, which gives the handler the object without copying. Each depth is followed by a call to the same extension. Pi's runtime gives the object at d=4300 and the RangeError at d=4400 on that Node; the row does not pin those, because the cut-off moves with the Node version.

## D269 A system fd or rg is accepted only if `--version` names it, and the `@` autocomplete runs fd in a new session

What: Pi's tools-manager `commandExists` runs `spawnSync(cmd, ["--version"], { stdio: "pipe" })` and counts any result but a spawn error as "installed", with no timeout, in Pi's own session. `getToolPath("fd")` therefore returns the first of `fd`, `fdfind` that spawns, and Pi runs it at startup (`ensureTool("fd")`) and on `@`. Debian's `fdclone` package installs a file manager as `/usr/bin/fd`; it opens `/dev/tty`, so Pi's `@` autocomplete, `find` and startup hand it the terminal. PiG's base only looked the name up (it never spawned), so it started FDclone at `@` and in `find`. PiG now:

| Step | Pi | PiG |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Candidate names | `fd`, then `fdfind` (rg: `rg`) | same |
| Acceptance of a PATH candidate | `spawnSync(cmd, ["--version"])` returns without a spawn error | `<bin> --version` exits 0 and stdout starts with `fd ` or `fdfind ` (rg: `ripgrep `); Debian's sharkdp build prints `fdfind 10.3.0` |
| On mismatch | the impostor wins | next name, then the managed download in `<agentDir>/bin` |
| The probe | Pi's session, no timeout | started like the searches (`nodespawn.SetProgram`), in a new session on unix or a console of its own without a window on Windows (`nodespawn.HideWindow`), stdin `/dev/null`, stdout a pipe; a 5 s bound (1 s pipe grace) that kills the process group on unix (the process on Windows); a completed verdict is cached per path, mtime and size, a timeout or start failure is not |
| `@` autocomplete fd search | Pi's session | new session on unix (a cancel kills the group); on Windows a console of its own without a window. `DETACHED_PROCESS` is not used: a Scoop or Chocolatey shim started under it opens a visible console window for the real program |
| `find` and `grep` fd/rg search | Pi's session | unchanged: the caller's session and group, so a terminal Ctrl+C in print mode reaches the search with the agent |

The managed copy in `<agentDir>/bin` is PiG's own download and is not probed.

Why: typing `@` over ssh on a host with fdclone launched a full-screen file manager, and a real fd installed as `fdfind` was never reached. Pi has the same bug.

Pi bug: `commandExists` in `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts` accepts any program that spawns, so an unrelated `fd` passes. To report upstream.

Scope: `tools.LookupToolPath` and `ToolsManager.GetToolPath` (so `EnsureTool`, `BeginEnsureTool` at startup through `ensureManagedTools`, the tools status line, and the `find`, `grep` and `@` lookups), and the `@` autocomplete's fd run. A system `fd` or `rg` whose `--version` line is not one of the prefixes (a wrapper, a custom build) is no longer used; it falls through to the managed download, or to nothing offline. Narrower than the first proposal: `find` and `grep` do not run their search in a new session, because the identity probe already keeps an impostor out and a new session would orphan rg and fd on a terminal Ctrl+C in print and json modes.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi checks that the program it found is the tool, not only that it spawns. Then the probe, the autocomplete's new session and this record go.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/tools/tool_probe.go` (`systemToolIdentity`, the 5 s probe bound, the 1 s pipe grace), `tui/file_autocomplete.go` (`walkDirectoryWithFd`).

Evidence: `TestLookupToolPathSkipsImpostorFdForFdfind`, `TestLookupToolPathRejectsLoneImpostor`, `TestLookupToolPathChecksRipgrepIdentity`, `TestLookupToolPathAcceptsFdWrapperWithoutShebang`, `TestToolProbeCachesPerFileRevision`, `TestProbeToolVersionTimesOutHungProgramAndItsChildren`, `TestProbeToolVersionDoesNotCacheStartFailure`, `TestVersionProbeCannotReachTheTerminal`, `TestFindAndGrepKeepSearchInCallersSession` (`internal/codingagent/tools/tool_probe_unix_test.go`, `tool_probe_linux_test.go`) and `TestWalkDirectoryWithFdRunsInANewSession`, `TestWalkDirectoryWithFdCancelKillsTheGroup` (`tui/file_autocomplete_session_linux_test.go`). The impostor and no-shebang cases fail on a45b578455, where a fake `fd` ahead of `fdfind` was returned.

Parity allowance: `TestLookupToolPathFindsToolsAsPiDoes` keeps comparing lookup order with Pi's `getToolPath` on Windows with fixtures that answer `--version` as the tool, so only the identity check differs.

## D270 After a /tree choice PiG holds its frames until the navigation returns; Pi paints the old transcript first

What: Pi's tree handler (interactive-mode.ts:5694-5775) awaits `session.navigateTree` (5750) and then runs `chatContainer.clear(); renderInitialMessages()` (5768). The key that closes the "Summarize branch?" prompt asks for an immediate frame (tui.ts:1158-1161) that runs on `process.nextTick` (tui.ts:1044-1058), before the microtask that resumes the await. So Pi paints the old transcript with the prompt gone first, and the fork point replaces it on the next frame: a few milliseconds without an extension handler, and the whole await of a `session_before_tree` handler that awaits. PiG's navigation runs on a worker and decodes the session's records for the context: on a 27 MB, 600-entry session of 88 KB tool results that is about 60 ms at 16 cores and about 190 ms at 4 (about 750 ms at 1) after the parallel read splits by record bytes (it was 830 ms at 16 before), and the owner saw the old transcript for about a second before the fork point. PiG now holds its frames from the choice that starts the navigation (No summary, Summarize, the custom prompt's submit, or the tree selection when `branchSummary.skipPrompt` is set) until the navigation has returned and the transcript is swapped, so the prompt stays on screen and the first frame after the choice is the fork point. The transcript's entries are read on the worker, so the repaint decodes nothing on the owner loop.

Why: the owner asked that the user never see the old leaf after selecting. Pi's old-transcript frame is brief enough not to be seen; PiG's decode is not always brief, and a hold is the only way to show no stale frame whatever the navigation takes.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: `/tree` navigation. The hold starts only from the choice of the prompt's own dialog. It ends when the navigation returns, when an extension opens a dialog or an overlay, when a summary shows its status, and when the run is aborted before navigating. Esc and the custom-prompt choice are not held. While it lasts, nothing that only requests a frame is painted: the user's typing, `setStatus`, `setWidget`, footer and header changes. A paint that names a render (`showStatus`, `showError`, notifications) still paints the old transcript with the prompt gone. The hold has no upper bound: a handler that awaits for a minute keeps the "Summarize branch?" prompt on screen for a minute, where Pi shows the old transcript.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: PiG's navigation completes within one frame for every session, or the owner decides to match Pi's brief old-transcript frame.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/interactive_tree.go` (`navigateTree`), `internal/codingagent/interactive.go` (`holdFrames`), `internal/codingagent/slash_session_handlers.go` (`treeNavigateWithSummarize`), `internal/codingagent/ext_ui_context.go` (`runDialog`).

Evidence: `TestTreeNavigationPaintsNoStaleFrameBeforeTheTranscriptSwap` (red without the hold: the editor painted during the navigation), `TestTreeSummaryPromptChoiceHoldsTheFramesOfItsClose`, `TestTreeDialogsOfOtherContextsNeverStartTheHold`, `TestTreeNavigationPaintsAnExtensionDialog` and `TestTreeNavigationPaintsAnExtensionOverlay` (`internal/codingagent/interactive_tree_frames_test.go`); scenario `tree/15-tree-navigation-no-stale-frame` (fails 42 of 119 sampled panes with `holdFrames` a no-op).

Parity allowance: scenario `tree/15-tree-navigation-no-stale-frame` runs a `session_before_tree` handler that awaits 600 ms. Both programs end on the same pane (`output_equal`). PiG's pane, sampled every 10 ms from the choice until "Navigated to selected point" shows, never shows the old leaf with the prompt gone (`pig_no_stale_frames`); Pi's samples are not asserted, because Pi shows that pane throughout the await.

## D272 The upgrade type check runs in a driver program that the go command builds from the staged SDK, not in the pig binary

What: `pig extension upgrade`, the startup SDK-drift offer and `extensionsAutoUpgrade` type check a Go extension with `go/types`. In Pi there is no SDK to drift from and no upgrade path, so no behavior of Pi is changed. In PiG the `pig` binary no longer links the type checker (`go/types`, `go/importer`, `go/printer`, `go/format` and the gc and gccgo importers: 511 KB of `go/types` symbols and about 1.4 MB of file). The type checker is the program `extensions/sdk/upgrade/driver`, a part of the staged Go SDK. `pig` runs `go run -trimpath ./upgrade/driver` in the staged SDK directory, with the go command and the environment that already list and build the extension. It sends the `LoadOptions` as JSON on standard input and reads the plan as JSON from standard output. The plan, the report, the diff, the exit codes and the error text are the same as before.

What a user can see:
- The analysis runs only when a Go source extension fails to build with an SDK drift error (the startup offer, `extension upgrade`, `extensionsAutoUpgrade`). Startup with no failing Go extension is not affected.
- Warm cache: +0.06 s to +0.2 s per analysis (`pig extension upgrade --dry-run`: 0.63 to 0.67 s before, 0.71 to 0.84 s after), and about +0.09 s per start that has a Go extension that fails to build (0.14 to 0.18 s before, 0.24 to 0.27 s after).
- The first analysis after a Go update, an SDK update or with an empty Go build cache compiles the driver: +4 s to +7 s (measured 4.2 to 4.7 s at start, 7.2 to 7.7 s for `extension upgrade`, 5.0 s on top of an empty build cache; go 1.27.3, linux/amd64, a loaded 16-CPU host, so ±10 %). The Go build cache keeps the driver, so later analyses do not pay it.
- It works offline: the SDK module has no requirement, so the driver build needs no download. The measurements ran with `GOPROXY=off GOTOOLCHAIN=local`.
- `pig` stages the Go SDK before the analysis (`EnsureSyncedLang`). A config root whose staged SDK cannot be written fails the analysis with the staging error. Before, the analysis ran from any SDK root the build found. A stock `pig` stages the SDK at startup already.
- A cancelled analysis closes the driver's standard input, which cancels the driver's `go list`.
- With no `go` on the path the text is the same as before. A Piglet Binary that compiled out the Go SDK cannot analyze a Go extension (D92).

Why: the type checker was 1.4 MB of a binary every user downloads, and only the upgrade path runs it. The go command is already required by that path, because it lists and builds the extension. The driver is compiled by the user's own toolchain, so the compiler export data and `go/types` match.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: the go command that the toolchain resolver picks, on every platform. The `pig` binary still links about 107 KB of `go/types` symbols, because the public package `extensions/sdk/upgrade` imports `go/types`; moving its analysis API out would be an incompatible change of the released SDK module and is not part of this record. The process tests of the driver run on Linux, macOS and Windows: the fake go command is the test binary itself (8c67993ab1). Only the CI jobs of macOS and Windows prove the same code on those systems. One `pig extension upgrade --dry-run` on the D109 fixture with a fresh `PIG_HOME`, rebuild included, takes 2.80 s before the driver and 3.17 s after (+0.37 s, 10 warm runs on xd670); `pig` peaks at 37.0 MB against 41.1 MB, and the driver child at about 12.6 MB.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the owner prefers the type checker in the binary again, or Go ships `go/types` in a form that the binary can link at no size cost.

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/extension_upgrade_analyze.go` (`analysisDriver`, `analysisKillDelay`, `analyzeExtension`, `runAnalysisDriver`), `extensions/sdk/upgrade/driver/main.go` (`main`).

Evidence: `coding/cli/extension_upgrade_driver_test.go` runs the real driver and the real go command for a fixture extension (the plan, the error text, cancel, a missing go command). `extensions/sdk/upgrade/driver/main_test.go` covers the request and reply round trip and the cancel by closing standard input. `strip_upgrade_link_test.go` requires that the binary does not link the type checker's packages. The upgrade tests of `extensions/sdk/upgrade` and `coding/cli` (`Upgrade`, `SDKDrift`, `AnalysisDriver`) pass with the driver in the path. Review `reviews/sizeclimb-sdk.md` measured the latencies above before and after on xd670.

## D274 A packed Go or Lisp member whose own code ends the shared process stops alone, and the members that shared the process restart and lose their in-memory state and in-flight calls

What: Pi loads every extension into one process, and an isolated PiG extension has a process of its own. A packed Go or Lisp cell shares one process among its members, and neither language can contain a failing thread of an extension's code in the process that serves the others: a goroutine one member started that panics, or a thread one member made that signals a condition nothing handles, ends the process. The SDKs recover what they can: a panic or condition in any callback of the extension's own code that an SDK runs on a goroutine or thread it started (a handler, a tool, a listener, an editor or overlay component, an MCP credential store's `WithRefreshLock`, a settings storage's `WithLock`, a compact or nested-call callback) is that call's error, in an isolated and in a packed process alike. What remains is code the extension itself started.

When such a thread ends the process, the host reads the trace the process wrote to its stderr log, only the trace of the panic or condition that ended it, and only when the process ended that way. A Go process that an unrecovered panic ended exits with status 2 and leaves the runtime's section: the last line of the log that begins `panic: `, its message and continuation lines, a blank line, and the header of the goroutine that was running (`goroutine N [running]:`), with nothing written between them and no `fatal error: ` line after it. The blocks the SDK writes for the panics it recovered (a handler's, a listener's) are handled failures and decide nothing. The frames of that goroutine, and its `created by` line, name a member when a frame's package is the member's import path, or belongs to the module of exactly one member. A Lisp backtrace (the last `Backtrace for:` section) counts only when SBCL's own `unhandled condition in --disable-debugger mode, quitting` line follows it, and names a member by the package that qualifies the function of a frame; the arguments SBCL prints after the function, which include the condition's text, name nobody. A trace that names exactly one member blames it. One that names two, or none, blames no one, and so does a process that ended any other way than by that panic or condition: `os.Exit`, a runtime fatal error (which ends with status 2 as a panic does, and writes `fatal error: `), `sb-ext:exit`, a kill. Whatever a member logged before decides nothing then: the text a member writes for a panic it handled in the form the Go runtime writes its own (`panic: ` and `debug.Stack()`), or a backtrace it prints (`sb-debug:print-backtrace` writes SBCL's `Backtrace for:` header), is not what ended the process. Four limits remain, and each blames or spares a member wrongly only in the case named: a Go member that logs such a block and then ends the process itself with `os.Exit(2)` is read as a panic of the member whose frames the block holds; and a Lisp thread of one member that calls another member's function in tail position names that other member, because SBCL's tail-call merging drops the calling frame from the backtrace (Go's `created by` line always names the starter, so Go is not affected). The other two need a trace crafted by the members' own code, written while the process is dying: a bare `debug.Stack()` block (it has no `panic: ` line) that a member writes between the blank line that follows the runtime's panic message and the runtime's goroutine header is read as the running goroutine's, and a backtrace a Lisp sibling prints between the culprit's backtrace and SBCL's quit line is read as the one that ended the process. The host then, when it blames one member,
- stops the old process and every member's connection, and quarantines no cell;
- stops the blamed member and reports it, with the cause the process wrote (`panic: ...`) and the log that holds it, as stopped until `/reload`, which is what an isolated extension's crash does; a `/reload` runs it isolated, in a process of its own, and never again beside the members it took down; and
- starts the other members again together in a new shared process, at once, and reports each of them as restarting.

The restarted members run their factories again, so an extension's in-memory state starts over, and a call that was in flight when the process ended fails with the connection error and is not replayed. An isolated extension's crash ends that extension only; here a sibling's crash restarts a member that did nothing wrong. A trace that names no member, or two (a kill, `os.Exit`, a runtime fatal error, a package two members share), stops every member of the cell until `/reload` restarts them, as before. A Rust thread that panics or a Python thread that raises ends that thread, not the process, so neither language reaches this path; a Node cell has its own recovery (D20).

Why: Before, one member's goroutine ended every sibling and told them to run `/reload`, though the trace says whose code it was. Stopping only the member whose code failed matches the isolation an isolated extension gives, and the one thing packing cannot give an in-process language, a boundary around a failing thread, is replaced by a restart of the others. Recovering inside the thread is not possible: a goroutine or thread that the extension started has no frame of the SDK to recover in.

Pi bug: none; Pi runs every extension in one process and has no process boundary to restart.

Scope: the Go and Lisp SDKs' packed cells (a placement of the same factory the isolated cell runs), when the process ends with a trace that names exactly one member of a cell of two or more. Not an isolated extension, not a Rust or Python cell, not a Node cell, not a process that ended any other way than by an unrecovered panic or an unhandled condition (`os.Exit`, a runtime fatal error, a signal).

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-07 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: an extension's goroutine or thread can be contained in the process that serves other extensions, or the owner decides that a crash of a packed member stops its siblings until `/reload`. Then the call-site marker, the trace readers and this record go.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/host/subprocess/packed_native_recovery.go` (`recoverNativeProcess`).

Evidence: `TestPackedProcessCrashBlamesTheMemberWhoseGoroutineEndedIt` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/packed_native_recovery_host_test.go`: a Go cell of two; the other member's handler had panicked and been recovered earlier; one member's goroutine panics; the other restarts, the culprit stops and a reload runs it isolated, no cell is quarantined, and only the culprit is reported stopped), `TestPackedProcessExitStopsTheWholeCell` (the same file: `os.Exit` after a recovered panic, and `os.Exit`, a fatal error and a kill after a logged panic block, blame no one and stop the whole cell), `TestPackedLispProcessEndedWithoutAnUnhandledConditionStopsTheWholeCell` (`test/extension-conformance/packed_crash_blame_test.go`: a printed backtrace, then `sb-ext:exit` or a kill), `TestGoTraceCulpritReadsOnlyWhatEndedTheProcess` and `TestLispTraceCulpritReadsOnlyWhatEndedTheProcess`, `TestPackedCrashBlameAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/packed_crash_blame_test.go`; Go and Lisp, isolated and packed), `TestGoTraceCulpritNamesTheMemberWhoseCodeEndedTheProcess` and `TestLispTraceCulpritNamesTheMemberWhosePackageQualifiesAFrame` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/packed_native_recovery_test.go`: recovered blocks, `os.Exit`, fatal errors, a condition's text and two named members), and for the callbacks the SDKs recover `extensions/sdk/callback_guard_test.go`, `TestEditorComponentFailingKeyIsReportedAndFinished` (`test/extension-conformance/editor_input_failure_test.go`) and `a-failing-compact-callback-does-not-end-the-process` (`extensions/sdk-lisp/tests/core-tests.lisp`).

Parity allowance: the row pins what a packed and an isolated placement share after one member's own thread ends its process: the blamed member is reported stopped with the log that holds its panic, the sibling answers a command and is never reported stopped, and a `/reload` brings both back. It does not pin that the sibling keeps its in-memory state, because it does not (the residue above).

## D277 A Node renderer component that handles the mouse itself takes the click only, and the host answers it handled for the whole component

What: Pi routes every mouse event of a fullscreen frame to the component under the pointer, and the answer of the component decides what the renderer does next (`tui-alt-screen.ts` `handleMouseEvent`, `handleSelectionMouseEvent`). A custom message or entry renderer of the Node SDK returns a component that lives in the extension's process, so the host cannot know its answer before it has to answer the terminal; it must not wait for the extension (AGENTS.md TUI rules). For a tree built from Pi's own `MouseRegion`, `Container` and `Box` the host needs no answer: the Node runtime finds the rectangles in which a `MouseRegion` answers by one walk over the tree that follows the three rules by which Pi's `dispatchMouseEvent` hands an event down (a `Container` and a `Box` to the child whose rows hold it, a `Box` inside its padding, a `MouseRegion` answering for every event that reaches it), sends them with the frame (`regions`), and the host hit-tests them. A click outside every region (the padding of a card) is left unhandled and falls through to text selection and copy-on-select; a click inside is handled and delivered to the component; Pi's compaction, branch-summary and skill cards and a `MouseRegion` card of an extension therefore match Pi byte for byte. The residue is a component whose tree has a handler that is not a `MouseRegion`, `Container` or `Box` (a hand-written `handleMouse`): PiG answers a click anywhere in it as handled at once and delivers it to the component (`dispatchMouseEvent`, coordinates local to it), and leaves every other event (press, release, drag, move, wheel) unhandled, so selection and scrolling work over it. A hand-written handler that takes press, drag, wheel, or a click only on some cells gets none of those events, and a click on a cell it would not have handled is handled and clears a text selection. A `MouseRegion` whose closure answers an event other than `click` (press, drag, wheel) gets only the click. A `MouseRegion` whose closure declines a left click (returns no result for it) is still answered handled, because the host cannot run the closure before it answers the terminal: the selection is cleared and copy-on-select is skipped, where Pi leaves the click unhandled. The SDKs whose renderers return a kit view are not affected: the host hit-tests a view's mouse regions from their description and answers exactly as the region says.

Why: asking the extension is impossible without waiting for it, and answering every event inside the component (D107 §4.3, the ui.custom rule) would make every clickable card swallow its text selection and the transcript's wheel scrolling.

Pi bug: none.

Observable effect: only an extension that writes its own `handleMouse`, a `MouseRegion` that answers press, drag or wheel, or a `MouseRegion` whose closure declines a click differs from Pi, in the cases above.

Scope: `render_message` and `render_entry` components of the Node runtime whose tree has a hand-written mouse handler, and the non-click events of a `MouseRegion` closure. Not tool renderers, widgets, header and footer (they take none yet, a recorded gap), not kit views, not trees of `MouseRegion`, `Container` and `Box` clicked.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-07, in this narrowed form only.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the host can ask the extension synchronously for a component's answer, or Pi's own components of a separate process need none.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/host/subprocess/render_proxy.go` (`HandleMouse`), `coding/extension/host/subprocess/runtime-node/runtime.mjs` (`renderTranscriptComponent`).

Evidence: `TestRenderProxyNodeComponentTakesClickOnly` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/render_proxy_mouse_test.go`) pins the residue; `TestRenderProxyNodeMouseRegionsAreHitTestedLikePi` and `TestRendererClickOutsideTheRegionFallsThrough` pin that a click outside the regions is unhandled and one inside is handled and counted; the tmux scenario `extensions-runtime/96-message-click-expansion` compares the clicks on the regions and on a card's padding with Pi's, but it cannot tell the narrowed behavior from the wide one (a padding click that the host wrongly handled still reaches Pi's own dispatch inside Node, which leaves it unhandled, so the screen is the same: only selection and copy-on-select differ, and a capture does not show them); the conformance row `TestRendererClickOutsideTheRegionFallsThrough` is the guard. `TestNodeMouseRegionsAreTheCellsPiDispatchReaches` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/node_mouse_regions_test.go`) pins the walk against Pi's own `dispatchMouseEvent` asked about every cell, over 600 generated trees of `Container`, `Box` with padding and `MouseRegion`, over layouts rendered at another width, and over a layout that changed after the render, and checks that no handler of the extension runs and a 4000-line card costs milliseconds (the per-cell probe took 1.8 s at width 100 and 2.9 s at width 200).

Parity allowance: none beyond the scenario above.

## D430 Go callers start a call and await it apart (Reference.Start, PendingCall) and run a handler's blocking remainder from outside the host package (AwaitingCtx, Resume)

What: Pi's `ref.method(args)` starts the call at once and returns a Promise. A caller may start several calls before awaiting any (Files v2's pipelined `append`s, which the call window allows) and the host accepts them in the order they were started. A blocking Go `Reference.Call` cannot express that, so `Reference.Start(method, args, ctx)` is the synchronous half (the call is admitted and its handler's synchronous part has run when it returns) and `PendingCall.Wait(ctx)` the awaiting half. `host.AwaitingCtx(ctx, work)` is `Awaiting` for a caller outside the package, which cannot name the host's loop: the blocking remainder of an `onLive` or `on` handler (the awaits of Pi's async function). `host.Resume(ctx, work)` is the other way for a Go caller to resume in the loop after a wait: it runs `work` as one synchronous section on the goroutine of a handler that suspended (a transaction, a blocking call), inline when the handler still runs in its admitting section, as the awaits of Pi's handler resume inside the loop.

Why: AGENTS.md "TypeScript async/Promise parity": an awaited Promise is a blocking call, but a Promise started and awaited later is two Go operations. Without Start the pipelined-append behaviour could not be tested or used through a reference; without AwaitingCtx a package outside `chord/next/host` (the app kit) could not declare an `onLive` routine.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: additive Go API of `chord/next/host`; no Pi behaviour changes.

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (draft; Pi main follow of 2026-11-07).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: never; it is the Go form of an un-awaited Promise and of a handler's async remainder.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/host/host_pending.go` (`AwaitingCtx`, `Resume`), `chord/nextnode/app/files.go` (`FilesFacet`), `chord/nextnode/app/files_support_test.go`.

Evidence: `TestChunkedUploadsWriteAppendsSentWithoutWaitingAtTheirOwnOffsetsByteForByte` and `TestChunkedUploadsFinishAnUploadOnlyOnceTheAppendsSentBeforeItLanded` (`chord/nextnode/app/files_test.go`), `TestChunkedUploadsDropTheUploadsAPreviousRunLeft` (the `onLive` routine), `TestResumeRunsAfterAHandlerSuspendedInASectionOfTheLoop` (`chord/next/host/host_resume_test.go`).

Parity allowance: none.

## D460 The app kit's facets are Go core facets beside the plugins' Node host

What: Pi loads the kit's facets and the plugins into one host. PiG's plugins run in a Node subprocess (`docs/plan/chord-plugins-node-evaluator.md`), so `StartServer`/`StartWorker` load the kit's facets (info, sessions, owner, pairing, files, themes, translations, telemetry ingest, the HTTP facet) as Go core facets on the server's Go host (a relay without an address on a worker); the plugins' `NodeHost` is a child link (`Accept` on the server, `WorkerPlugins.Load` on a worker). The local types plugins use (`AppInfo`, `Sessions`, `WorkerInfo`) reach the Go objects through `plugins.BridgedFacet`. `HostData` must be served at the worker's address, which the NodeHost serves, so it is a JavaScript core facet (`host_data.mjs`, written per start with the server's principal and a secret token) forwarding `exportTo` to the bridged Go local `chord.app.data.export`; plugins can see the bridged type but cannot call it without the token (in Pi they cannot reach the facet at all).

Why: the plugin runtime is Node; the facets are Go because the kit is a Go port. Moving them to the Node host would port the kit to JS; serving HostData from Go needs the Go relay to answer for its child's address, which a chord host does not do.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/app`, `chord/nextnode/plugins` (bridge).

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08, OWNER-DECISIONS #19.

Remove when: the plugins' host and the kit's facets run in one process (the plugin runtime is not a subprocess), or a chord host can answer for its child's address so `HostData` needs no JavaScript module.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/app/app.go` (`start`), `chord/nextnode/app/worker.go` (`runWorker`), `chord/nextnode/app/data.go` (`hostDataModuleText`).

Evidence: `app_test.go`, `app_workers_test.go`, `app_export_test.go`.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D461 `Files` and `FilesFolder` are not reachable from TypeScript plugins

What: `Files` has a keyed member (`summary`), and the plugin bridge refuses contracts with keyed members, so a TS plugin cannot `get` a host's files; Go code can. The bridge could forward it only by mirroring the Go documents into a non-persisted keyed document handle of the proxy object (a JS keyed member is a document handle or a reference's keyed member; the Go reference's `Keyed.Get/Watch` exist, the proxy side does not), which means an invented key-pin protocol between two hosts; not done.

Why: the plugins' proxy facets (`plugins/node/proxy.mjs`) forward state members and methods to the Go object; a JS keyed member must be a keyed document handle or a reference's keyed member, and the proxy has neither for an object on another host. Pi has the kit and the plugins in one host, so `self.get(Files, {})` works there.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/app` (the Files facet), `chord/nextnode/plugins` (the bridge refuses a contract with a keyed member at start). Observable: a TypeScript plugin loses Pi's public `chord/app/files` API (`Files`, `FilesFolder`); Go code and the kit's own routes keep them. Pi's own p2 plugins call `self.get(Files, {})`; PiG's p2 lane ports them to Go, so p2 itself is not affected.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: accepted for 0.8.0 by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08, OWNER-DECISIONS #19: the limitation stands.

Remove when: the plugin bridge forwards a contract with a keyed member (a keyed-handle proxy between hosts), or the plugins run in the kit's host.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/app/app.go` (`start`), `chord/nextnode/app/worker.go` (`runWorker`).

Evidence: `plugins_node_bridged_keyed_test.go` (the refusal); `files_test.go` (the facet from Go).

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08 (accepted limitation for 0.8.0)

## D462 The server and each worker have two stores, not one

What: Pi's host has one store and one commit queue. PiG has two hosts per server or worker (the Go host, the plugins' NodeHost), and a store has one writer (a singleton meta row: `last_seq`, `next_id`), so each has its own: the kit's (`kit.sqlite`, `store.sqlite` on a server) and the plugins' (`store.sqlite`, `plugins.sqlite` on a server). Nothing commits across them. Archives carry an optional `kit.archive` beside `store.archive`. A running worker's export (`PackRunning`) takes the kit's commit queue (its export's first part), then has SQLite copy the plugins' file at one instant (`VACUUM INTO` over a read-only connection, a read transaction the NodeHost's writes do not disturb) and exports the copy, so the two archives are one point; the kit's writes wait for its archive, the plugins' writes proceed meanwhile and are not in the archive. Pi holds its one store's commit queue. The copy costs the plugins' store's size in temporary space beside the archive.

Archive interop, both directions (accepted incompatibilities; owner to decide whether to build a converter): PiG to Pi: Pi's `unpackEntries` refuses any entry besides the manifest, `store.archive` and `files/`, so Pi cannot import a PiG app-kit archive that has `kit.archive`. Pi to PiG: a Pi archive's single `store.archive` holds the kit's Files tables beside the plugins' records and is imported into the plugins' store; the Go Files facet reads the kit store, which stays empty, so the files arrive (`files/`) but their index does not. [INFERENCE: the second direction is read from the code, not run with a Pi-made archive.] Options if the owner wants interop: split the kit-owned tables into `kit.sqlite` on import, or export a single Pi-compatible archive.

Why: the node-evaluator design (`docs/plan/chord-plugins-node-evaluator.md` §store): the Go store is an in-process engine and a store is not shared over the link; sharing one sqlite file between two engines would corrupt the id allocator.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08, OWNER-DECISIONS #19.

Remove when: the plugins run in the server's (or worker's) own host process with one store, or one store file can be shared by two hosts.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/app/app.go` (`openServer`), `chord/nextnode/app/worker.go` (`runWorker`), `chord/nextnode/app/archive.go` (`PackRunning`).

Evidence: `app_export_test.go`, `archive_upstream_test.go` (PiG round trip only), `data_export_test.go` (both stores written during 8 exports: each archive holds exactly the rows its manifest counts, and the plugins' ticks are the kit's or one more).

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D464 FormatNumber and FormatTime take closed option types and return errors (P1-3)

What: Pi passes `Intl.NumberFormatOptions` / `Intl.DateTimeFormatOptions` through to `Intl`. Go has no ICU, so the output comes from tables generated by running the pinned Node (v24.22.1, ICU 78.3, CLDR 48) over every locale ICU has data for (`automation/gen/generate-intl-tables.mjs`, `make intl-tables`, part of `pin-bump`; `chord/next/surfaces/intl_gen.go`). The tables cover a closed set: `NumberOptions` (minimum/maximum fraction digits, 0 to 100) and `TimeOptions` (`timeStyle` short|medium, or any of hour/minute/second numeric|2-digit, with optional `hour12`): what Pi's call sites pass (none, `{timeStyle: "short"}`, `{hour: "numeric"}`) and their siblings. Anything else (time zone, zone names, calendars, eras, fractional seconds, day periods, `hourCycle`, currency/percent/compact/unit, significant digits, rounding modes, Unicode locale extensions such as `-u-nu-arab`) cannot be expressed; a Unicode extension in the language's locale returns `ErrLocale`. Both functions return `(string, error)` for what Intl throws a RangeError/TypeError for (min > max fraction digits, an option out of range, an ill-formed tag, an empty option set that would print a date, a style with fields) as `ErrOption` / `ErrLocale`; before, min > max was silently coerced. An unavailable or aliased locale resolves as Intl does (CLDR aliases, a measured exceptions table, BestAvailableLocale, then the platform default).

Why: a hand table or x/text would be a different function: x/text is CLDR 32 and half-even (312 differing outputs in the review's probe, 75 with no options), and has no date patterns. The generator derives symbols, affixes, grouping (including minimumGroupingDigits), numbering system digits, hour cycle, padding and day periods from `formatToParts` and refuses to emit unless its renderer reproduces `format()` byte for byte over a corpus (942 number locales x 10 option sets x 59 values; every time locale x every shape x 24 hours x 6 minute/second pairs).

Root data and aliases (RevChordIntl P3-3, P3-4): when neither a tag nor the platform's locale has data, ICU formats in its root locale. The generator observes it in child Nodes under `LANG=xx_<REGION>` and `LC_ALL=` (the process's default locale is read from the environment): the numbers equal en-US's (checked over the same corpus), the times follow the region's hour cycle (221 regions have a row of their own, the rest and no region share another) and a variable set to the empty string is the locale `und` with its own row. Compound legacy aliases (`hy-arevmda` is `hyw`, `zh-hakka` is `hak`, `zh-xiang` is `hsn`) are probed from `Intl.getCanonicalLocales` over language x variant. Not modeled: root data for a platform tag that carries a script or variant with its own cycle, and `-u-` value aliases.

Scope: `chord/next/surfaces`.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08 (OWNER-DECISIONS #7, after the review's wording fixes).

Remove when: Go has an `Intl` with the pinned Node's CLDR, or a Pi call site passes an option outside the closed types (the types then grow and the generator with them).

Call-site markers: `chord/next/surfaces/surfaces_format.go` (`FormatNumber`), `chord/next/surfaces/intl_time.go` (`FormatTime`).

Evidence: `TestNumbersMatchNode`, `TestTimesMatchNode` (random corpus against a live Node, errors included; `INTL_DRIFT_CASES`, `INTL_DRIFT_SEED`), `TestFormatsNumbersLikeTheOracle`, `FuzzFormatNumber` (against an independent decimal reference), `FuzzFormatTime`, benchstat in the READY line.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D465 FormatTime takes a time.Time, not `Date`, `number` or `string`

What: Pi's `new Date(at)` accepts a `Date`, epoch milliseconds or an ISO/date string (`new Date(string)`'s parser, with its engine-defined formats). Go takes a `time.Time`; a caller converts milliseconds (`time.UnixMilli`) or parses its own string. The zone is `time.Local` (the process's TZ, as a `Date`'s default is), whatever location `at` carries. An invalid `Date` (a RangeError in Pi) cannot be expressed, and an instant outside the JS Date range (±8.64e15 ms), a RangeError in Pi, is formatted. Zone data: Go reads the system zoneinfo (or the embedded `time/tzdata`), Node uses ICU's bundled tz data with backzone history. Instants before 1970 in zones that tzdata links to another (Europe/Amsterdam before 1937: Go prints Brussels' LMT, Node Amsterdam's) and a host whose tzdata release differs from Node's bundled one can print different clock times (RevChordIntl P3-5: 0 mismatches in 19 other zones around every 2025-2027 transition).

Scope: `chord/next/surfaces`.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08 (OWNER-DECISIONS #7, after the review's wording fixes).

Remove when: never for the `time.Time` parameter (it is the Go form of a `Date`); the zone-data difference goes when Go's tzdata and the pinned Node's ICU zone data agree.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/surfaces/intl_time.go` (`FormatTime`).

Evidence: `TestFormatTimeShowsTheInstantInTheProcessZone`.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D466 No per-language formatter cache

What: Pi keeps a language's default `Intl.DateTimeFormat`/`NumberFormat` in a `WeakMap<Language, Formatters>` because a formatter costs far more than a format. Go formats from static tables with a binary search per call (no formatter object), so the "are collected with the language" case has nothing to test and is designed out. A canonical tag (what `Language.Locale` holds) allocates only the result string (one allocation per call); a non-canonical tag or the platform default locale allocates up to six times to canonicalize it (`EN-us` 5, `sh-RS` 3, `de-DE-1996` 2, the platform default under LANG=de_DE.UTF-8 6, measured by RevChordIntl P3-6).

Scope: `chord/next/surfaces`.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08 (OWNER-DECISIONS #7, after the review's wording fixes).

Remove when: never: Go has no formatter object to cache; it goes if a format stops being a table lookup.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/surfaces/surfaces_format.go` (`FormatNumber`).

Evidence: `TestFormatsAllocateOnlyTheResultForCanonicalTags`.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D501 `npm pack --json` printing an object (npm 12) is read like the list npm 10 and 11 print

What: Pi's install reads `const [tarball] = JSON.parse(output)`, the list of packed tarballs npm 10 and 11 print. Newer npm (12) prints an object keyed by package name, which that destructuring cannot read: with npm 12 Pi's packing fails. PiG's `unpack` reads the first member of such an object the same way it reads the first element of the list, so an install works with either npm.

Why: a plugin install that fails on a current npm for a reason of output shape only is worse than Pi's behavior and costs nothing to avoid; the list form, the empty-output errors and the `filename` read are Pi's.

Pi bug: With npm 12 Pi's `installPlugin` fails on the object output; to be filed upstream with the repro (`npm pack --json` of any package under npm 12).

Scope: `chord/nextnode/plugins` (`packedTarball`, used by `unpack`).

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08 (OWNER-DECISIONS #2).

Remove when: Pi's install reads the object form.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_install.go` (`packedTarball`).

Evidence: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_install_test.go` (`TestPackedTarballReadsTheListNpm10And11PrintAndTheObjectNpm12Prints`).

Parity allowance: observable only with npm 12, where Pi's packing fails.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D502 `TestPlugin` tests a plugin on the production topology: its host is the Node subprocess and the stubs are Go facets, not JavaScript values handed to one in-process host

What: Pi's `testPlugin(dir, { kind, modules, stubs, ... })` (`packages/chord/src/node/plugins/testing.ts`) builds the plugin, evaluates it in the test's own process with live JavaScript `modules`, loads it with live `stubs` (`PluginFacet[]`) on one new in-memory host and returns that host. PiG's `TestPlugin` (`chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_testing.go`) builds with the same `BuildFacet` and options and the same discovery, but a plugin evaluates only in the Node subprocess (AGENTS.md: no embedded JavaScript runtime; `docs/plan/chord-plugins-node-evaluator.md`), so: `Modules` are files the subprocess imports (the app's API as modules on disk, as a production host's `HostPackages`), not instances; a stub is a Go facet on a Go host (`TestPluginOptions.Stubs`, bridged to the plugin's host as `BridgedFacet` is, so it serves local types without keyed members) or a JavaScript module (`TestPluginOptions.Core`, which may also serve or contribute to registries and serve remote types); the result holds two hosts, the plugin's `NodeHost` (`TestedPlugin.Host`, reached through `NodeHost.Get` and `NodeHost.Hold`, the Go forms of `host.get` and `host.hold` for a local type) and the stubs' Go host (`TestedPlugin.Stubs`).

Why: a live JavaScript closure, module instance or `Host` cannot cross the process boundary, and the alternative (a second evaluator of plugins in the Go process) is forbidden. The topology is the one apps run (a Go host with a Node host as its child), so a plugin that passes `TestPlugin` meets its stubs through the same bridge it meets Go core facets through in production.

Scope: `TestPlugin`, `TestPluginOptions`, `TestStub`, `TestedPlugin.Host` and `Stubs`; the cases of `testing.test.ts` and example 52 give the same inputs (plugin files, API, contracts) and the same expectations (values read, contributions, names listed, errors) through these forms. Behaviour that is Pi's: a facet that throws while it evaluates rejects with that error as it is (`*NodeError`), and a setup that throws is `testPlugin: setup failed: ...` (`TestTestPluginRejectsAScriptThatThrowsWhileItEvaluatesWithItsOwnError`); the build and its refusals (`failed to build`), the versioned-API check, the root and kind derivation (single-file and checkout plugins, kinds named by files, `*.test.*`/`*.d.ts` naming none), the collecting facet and its fixed point, the wait for activations bounded by `timeouts.ready`, an in-memory store.

Owner decision: approved 2026-11-08 (option (a), port with a Go `TestPlugin`, 2026-11-08 00:36Z; this record is the residue). One two-host effect: a Go stub whose setup fails makes `TestPlugin` fail on the stubs' Go host before the plugin loads, so the error lists only the stub's failure; Pi loads stubs and plugin in one `host.load` and joins both failures in one "setup failed" message.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_testing.go` (`TestStub`, `TestPlugin`), `chord/nextnode/plugins/node/testing.mjs`.

Evidence: `TestTestPlugin*` (`chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_testing_test.go`, the cases of `testing.test.ts`) and `TestExample52TestPlugin` (`plugins_examples_testplugin_test.go`).

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the forms above; the Pi cases pass with Go stubs, file modules and the two hosts.

Remove when: Pi evaluates plugins out of process (its stubs then need a wire form too) or PiG gets an in-process evaluator.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D503 `TestPlugin`'s registries are named, its contributions are data, and `root` is `RootPrincipal`: no JavaScript closure or registry token crosses

What: Pi's `TestedPlugin.contributions(R)` takes the registry token `R` and returns its entries with their live values, and `root(fn, context, options)` runs the closure `fn` as the host's principal or `options.principal`. PiG's `TestedPlugin.Contributions(name, ctx)` takes the registry's name and returns `[]Contribution{Plugin, Type, Version, Value}` with each value as JSON data (a function or other non-data value in a contribution does not cross; the control channel's value form carries `bytes()` leaves), and `RootPrincipal(principal, ctx)` runs the fixed closure `async (ctx) => ctx.principal` of Pi's own test on the plugin's host and answers the principal its context has (the host's own for nil). The error for a registry that is not collected is Pi's message (`testPlugin: registry <name> is not collected ...`).

Why: the plugin's host is in the Node subprocess; a Go function cannot run there as an object's code, and a JavaScript closure written in a Go test is not a Go value. The helper's own reads (the collection's fixed point over `undeclaredRegistries`, `activationsUnderWay`, `timeouts.ready`, `unservedNames`, the reload that adds the collecting facet) run in the subprocess (`node/testing.mjs`), so the host's internals never cross; only their results do.

Scope: `TestedPlugin.Contributions`, `RootPrincipal`, `Unserved`. A test that needs `ctx.get` of other objects as another principal has no Go form of `root` yet; it reads through `NodeHost.Get` as the host's principal.

Owner decision: approved 2026-11-08 (same option as D502).

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_testing.go` (`Contributions`, `RootPrincipal`), `chord/nextnode/plugins/node/testing.mjs`.

Evidence: `TestTestPluginLoadsThePluginBuiltAgainstTheAPIBesideAStubCollectsItsContributionsAndListsWhatItWaitsFor` (`plugins_testing_test.go`: contributions by name, root as the host and as `u2`), `TestExample52TestPlugin`.

Parity allowance: none needed.

Remove when: PiG's plugin host can run a Go closure in the plugin's host process, or Pi's `root` takes data.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D520 A connection reset as soon as it is accepted reports the response read, where Pi usually reports the connect

What: a server that resets every connection the moment it accepts it makes the pinned pi-ai report `Connection error.: connect ECONNRESET <addr>` on `openai-completions`, `openai-responses` and `anthropic-messages` (60 runs against the same Go-hosted server: `connect ECONNRESET <addr>` 56, `write ECONNRESET` 4, `read ECONNRESET` 0). PiG reports `Connection error.: read ECONNRESET` in most runs (`connect ECONNRESET <addr>` 2, `write ECONNRESET` 4, `read ECONNRESET` 54). The cause is always kept; only the operation named in it differs.

Why: Go's dial returns before the peer's reset arrives and the request write goes into the socket buffer, so the reset surfaces on the response read. That is the same observable state as a peer that resets after it read the request, where Pi reports `read ECONNRESET` (6/6 probes) and PiG matches. PiG cannot tell the two apart without guessing from timing, and the reset after the request is the failure a real server produces, so it keeps Node's text for that case.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: `nodeConnectionFailure` (`ai/errors.go`): the connection-error cause of the three streaming APIs and the OpenAI SDK transport.

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (drafted by the FollowAi lane on 2026-11-08 at the reviewer's request, reviews/follow-ai3.md).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: PiG can tell a reset on accept from a reset after the request (for example by recording whether the request body was written before the reset reached the connection), or Pi stops reporting the connect.

Call-site markers: `ai/errors.go` (`nodeConnectionFailure`).

Evidence: `TestConnectionErrorKeepsCauseThroughSDKsMatchesPi` (`ai/connection_cause_oracle_test.go`: refused, reset after the request, closed after the request, closed on accept, all exact against the pinned pi-ai) and `TestOpenAISDKTransportErrorConnectionReset` (`ai/openai_sdk_transport_error_test.go`: reset on accept accepts `connect`, `write` or `read`).

Parity allowance: the reset-on-accept case accepts the three operations because the pinned pi-ai reaches any of them by timing and PiG reaches the read by construction.

## D521 A bigint in validated tool arguments is the string `<digits>n`, where Pi holds a JavaScript bigint

What: a tool schema with `Type.BigInt` (`{"type":"bigint"}`) coerces a JSON argument to a bigint in Pi's `validateToolArguments` (`packages/ai/src/utils/validation.ts`), and Pi's own `validation-probes.test.ts` writes that value as `${value}n` because `JSON.stringify` throws on a bigint (Pi af79b0c3d). PiG hands the validated arguments to a tool as JSON text, so a coerced bigint is written as the JSON string `"<digits>n"` (`ai.appendStringify`), and the four probe rows that hold a bigint or an `undefined` (bigint x3, undefined) record that representation. A tool that reads the argument as a number or as a string without the suffix sees the suffixed string, where Pi's tool receives a bigint.

Why: Go has no JavaScript bigint inside a JSON document and a tool argument reaches the tool, the transcript and the extension protocols as JSON. A bigint cannot be written as a JSON number without losing the type or the digits it carries (`JSON.stringify` has no form for it), and a reader of the text cannot tell a bigint from a number. The string keeps every digit and marks the type the way Pi's probes do. `undefined` is not a divergence: it is no member of an object and `null` in an array, as `JSON.stringify` writes it.

Pi bug: none; Pi's tool receives a bigint in process. The probes' `${value}n` is Pi's own notation for it.

Scope: `appendStringify` (`ai/validate.go`) and so `ValidateToolArguments`: only a schema that names the `bigint` type, which no stock tool and no model catalog schema uses; an MCP or extension tool with `type: "bigint"` is the only way to reach it.

Owner decision: owner approved option D (keep the string representation, record it as a numbered divergence) 2026-11-08 (owner list #22, "proceed with recommendations").

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the tool-argument path carries a typed bigint (for example a JSON extension with a numeric tag) that every reader of the arguments understands.

Call-site markers: `ai/validate.go` (`appendStringify`, the `*big.Int` case).

Evidence: `TestValidationProbesMatchPi` and `TestValidateToolArgumentsMatchesPiOnPiProbes` (`ai/validate_probes_upstream_test.go`, `ai/validate_pi_oracle_test.go`): the bigint rows of `ai/testdata/validation-probes.json` run against Pi's recorded outcome and against Pi's own `validateToolArguments`, whose oracle (`ai/testdata/validate_tool_args.mjs`) writes a bigint as `${value}n`.

Parity allowance: the four bigint and undefined rows of `validation-probes.test.ts` are compared by Pi's outcome notation, which PiG's representation matches; a tool that needs the bigint type gets the suffixed string.

## D540 The plugins watcher's directory identity has no birth time on a system whose stat Go does not expose it for

What: Pi renews the base watches under an entry on the way to base code only when the entry's identity, its inode and birth time (`identityOf`, `watcher.ts`), differs from the one it had when watched. PiG does the same with `statx` (ino, btime) on Linux and Android, `lstat` (ino, birthtimespec) on macOS, FreeBSD and NetBSD, `lstat` (ino, `X__st_birthtim`) on OpenBSD, and the file index and creation time on Windows. On any other Unix (DragonFly, Solaris and illumos, AIX) Go's `syscall.Stat_t` has no birth time, so the identity is the inode alone: a directory removed and made again that is given the same inode number is not seen as replaced there.

Why: the birth time is not in Go's `syscall` on those systems, and nothing in PiG ships for them (the release matrix is Linux, Android, macOS and Windows). Linux file systems with no birth time (tmpfs) give 0 in Pi as well (libuv's `statx`), so they are not part of this record.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_watcher_identity_other.go`, the `base` mark of `pluginWatcher` on DragonFly, Solaris and illumos, and AIX.

Owner decision: owner sign-off pending (drafted by the FollowChordPlugins lane on 2026-11-08).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: PiG ships for such a system and its `Stat_t` carries a birth time, or Go's `syscall` gives one.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_watcher_identity_other.go`.

Evidence: `TestIdentityOfADirectoryIsItsInodeAndBirthTimeAndNoneForNoEntry` states the identity on the systems tested; the build of the file is checked by `GOOS=dragonfly go vet ./chord/nextnode/plugins` (and netbsd, openbsd for the files that read a birth time).

Parity allowance: none needed; no Pi test depends on an inode reused with no birth time.

## D541 A message object is one per Node process and one per ExtensionRunner call; Pi's extensions and callers all share one realm

What: In Pi every extension and every caller runs in one JavaScript realm, so the message of `message_start`, `message_end`, `turn_end` and `agent_end`, the session entry's `message` and anything passed on `pi.events` are one object. PiG shares that object between every handler and every extension packed in one Node process, between the events of the Session's own run and the session entry, between the handlers of one `ExtensionRunner` emit (Go, Rust, Python and Node callers alike), and across Node processes for a message passed on `pi.events` (the receiver that holds the message resolves it to its own object). What it cannot share:

| Case | Pi | PiG |
|---|---|---|
| A Go, Python or Rust caller of `ExtensionRunner.emitMessageEnd` (or `emit`, `emitBoundary`) passes a message it built or read from `getEntries()`, and compares it with the `event.message` a Node handler holds, or a later emit's | `===` when it is the same object | false: the caller passes a value (a Go map, a Python dict, a Rust `Value`), and `getEntries` returns a fresh decode on each call, so there is no object a number could name |
| A Node handler of `ExtensionRunner.emitMessageEnd` edits `event.message` in place | the caller's object holds the edit | the edit is made to the message decoded for the call; the caller sees it only in a replacement the result carries |
| Node extensions in two processes (isolated cells, a member quarantined after a fault, different runtimes): one edits its message in place after the other holds it | both hold the one object, so the edit is on it | each process holds its own object for the message, with its own edits; a later payload that carries the host's value does not undo an edit of the process's own, and a `message_end` handler's edit goes back to the host and reaches the other process as a new value |
| A handler of `message_start`, `turn_end` or `agent_end` edits the message in place | the saved entry holds the edit | the edit stays in the extension process's object and is not saved: only a `message_end` handler's edit goes back to the host (its reply carries the whole message) |
| An extension passes a message on `pi.events` to a Node extension in another process that has not yet received that message's `message_end` | `===` its own message later | a proxy to the sender's object, as for any object on the bus (D83); the receiver's own object, once it receives the message, is another |
| A Node extension whose only message handlers are `turn_end` or `agent_end` compares `agent_end.messages[i]` with `entry.message` | `===` | false: the host numbers a message when a Node handler receives `message_start` or `message_end`, and persists it before any later handler asks |

Why: A JavaScript object cannot cross a process boundary, and a value passed by a Go, Python or Rust caller has no identity. The host numbers each message (`RequestPayload.message_seqs`, from one process-wide counter, so two Sessions or runners never share a number in a Node process), and a Node process resolves a number to the object it holds: that carries identity wherever the host holds a message object (a Session's run, a graph emit's decoded event) and wherever a Node process holds the number (`pi.events`). The rows above are what has no such object or no such process. Matching packed to isolated would remove behavior Pi has.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: `ExtensionRunner` of the Go, Rust and Python SDKs (`sessionGraph.new` kind `ExtensionRunner`) over Node extensions, for the first two rows; Node extensions in different processes and edits made outside `message_end`, for the next three; Node extensions that handle neither `message_start` nor `message_end`, for the last. Not: Node extensions packed in one process; the Session's own run seen by one extension; a Node caller (it uses Pi's own in-process ExtensionRunner, D73); other SDKs' payloads, which are unchanged.

Owner decision: 2026-11-08, owner list item #8 (isolated message identity) approved as written, in the narrowed form; proposed by PaseoIdentity and narrowed after the review in `reviews/paseo-identity-f4.md` (the per-emit numbering and `pi.events` resolution the first draft listed here are ported).

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

Remove when: Node extensions share one process or realm (the isolated placement goes), the Go, Python and Rust SDKs pass messages by reference to an object the host holds (a handle API), or a `message_end` handler's in-place edit goes back to the host of a graph emit and a message edited outside `message_end` is sent back and saved. Then the call-site markers and this record go.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/host/subprocess/runtime-node/runtime.mjs` (`messageNumber`), `coding/extension/host/subprocess/host.go` (`makeEventHandler`: the `MessageSeqs` block), `coding/session_graph_extension_runner_emit.go` (`withEmitMessageSequences` and the `emitMessageEnd` case of `callEmit`).

Evidence: `TestNodeRuntimeKeepsAnEditMadeOutsideMessageEnd` and `TestNodeRuntimeEntryBindWithReorderedMembersKeepsAnEdit` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/runtime_node_event_message_identity_test.go`; an edit outside `message_end` stays in the object and is not saved), `TestXrefMessageCrossesByNumberToTheReceiversOwnObject` (same file; a receiver that does not hold a number gets a proxy), `TestPiEventsMessageIsTheReceiversOwnObject` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/xref_message_identity_test.go`; packed and isolated), the `a_identity` entry of the `extension_runner` row (`test/extension-conformance/extension_runner_test.go`; two handlers of one emit hold one object, isolated and packed, in every SDK), `TestNodeExtensionFindsTheEntryOfItsMessagesAfterAnInPlaceEdit` (`coding/session_message_identity_node_test.go`).

Parity allowance: none; each row above is a divergence of Pi's one realm, and the rows PiG matches are pinned by the tests above.

## D601 The browser page's and the terminal sandbox's client runtimes, and the React bindings, are Pi's TypeScript shipped unchanged, not a Go port

What: Pi's `@earendil-works/chord/web` (`startPage`, `pageEnvironment`, `followLanguage`, `followThemes`, `PageOptions`), `@earendil-works/chord/react` (`HostProvider`, `useHold`, `useHost`, `useMessages`, `usePageState`, `useRefState`, `useRegistry`, `useWatch`, `watchStore`, `ExternalStore`) and the sandbox side of `@earendil-works/chord/tui` (`Bridge`, `SandboxChannel`, `BridgeTerminal`, `TerminalRuntime`, `TerminalRuntimeOptions`, `startTerminalRuntime`) have no Go declaration. PiG embeds Pi's own source files for them in the server (`chord/nextnode/surfaces/client`: `pi/chord/src/{web,react,tui}`, React 19.3.0 beside them, the terminal sandbox's bootstrap) and serves them to the browser and to the Node sandbox the Go launcher starts.

Why: these modules run in the user's browser (DOM, `window`, React) or in a JavaScript context the launcher starts (the app's terminal code and the plugins' registered scripts are evaluated there). A Go process cannot be that runtime, so there is nothing to port; the faithful form is the file itself. Behavior equals Pi's because the code is Pi's.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/surfaces/client` (vendored tree, `manifest.json`, `automation/gen/vendor-chord-surfaces-client.sh`), the interface rows of `pkg:chord/web`, `pkg:chord/react` and the sandbox half of `pkg:chord/tui` (designed out in `test/parity/interface-closure/autobind/reviewed-decisions.json`).

Owner decision: approved 2026-11-08 (Pi's chord web/react/tui-sandbox client modules ship as Pi's TypeScript unchanged; `client_manifest_test.go` checks their hashes). Drafted by the ChordGapTui lane.

Remove when: PiG runs the browser page or the terminal sandbox from Go-authored code.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/surfaces/client/client.go`.

Evidence: `TestReactWatchStoresReadTheValueCallBackOnChangesGiveUndefinedForNoWatchable` (`chord/nextnode/surfaces`), `TestReactBindingsHoldWhileMountedReRenderOnChangeReleaseOnUnmount`, `TestThePagesLanguageWritesLangAndDirOnTheRootElementFromTheDeviceThenTheUsersPick` (`chord/nextnode/app`, real Chromium), `chord/nextnode/tui/tui_kit_upstream_test.go`.

Parity allowance: none needed: Pi's tests for these modules run over the shipped files.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D605 The server's two hosts serve one union: `Host.Delegate` one way, the Node host's destination the other

What: Pi's server runs its plugins in its own host (`createHost({ address: Server() })` loads them), so the server serves the union of the kit's types and the plugins' from one place. The Go kit's plugins run in a Node subprocess behind the Go server host, which is also addressed `server` (it serves the kit's own remote types: the locale and theme choices, telemetry ingest, the app's Go facets). Both directions are kept so a hold binds on whichever of the two serves the type. Go to Node: `Host.Delegate(kind, RouteOptions, ctx)` is the route of the host's own address kind, consulted only for remote types placed on the host that its published table does not serve: those holds and references go over the delegate (a relayed hold, a remote object, parked while `Resolve` runs, resolved again once after a drop), everything the host serves stays here whatever the state of its object. A hold arriving on the delegate's own link for a type nobody serves is answered `unavailable` (not sent back). The app kit gives the NodeHost the address `server` and delegates to its link instead of accepting it. Node to Go: Pi's `Destination` places every remote type of the NodeHost's own address here, served or not, so a plugin's `self.get(LocaleChoice, {})` would wait `<type> is not served here` for ever. The Node host runs Pi's `Destination` through `node/destination.mjs` (host.ts's import of `./destination.ts` is answered by `resolve.mjs`; Pi's file is vendored as it is): for a remote type placed here that the Go host serves (`Host.Serves`, asked over a synchronous callback, `RuntimeLoad.ParentServes`, kept until the next load or reload) the reference goes over the parent link, where the Go host's own object, whatever its variants and members, binds. Residual difference: a hold over that link acts as the link's principal, the server's (a child cannot claim to be someone else, link §4: `callerOf`), where Pi's one host acts as the plugin object's own principal. So a plugin object's LocaleChoice or ThemeChoice is the server principal's, not the page owner's. Making it the object's principal needs the child's link to name whom a hold acts for, which Pi's protocol allows only from a parent to its child; it is not done here and the test pins today's behaviour.

Why: process boundary (a Node subprocess for the plugins) and the Go server's two hosts (D462). Without the first direction a TypeScript server plugin cannot serve a remote type (`a host without an address serves only local types`, or, with the address, `unavailable`), which every reload row on the server needs; without the second a server plugin cannot hold any type the Go server serves.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: `chord/next/host` (`Host.Delegate`, `Host.Serves`, `destination`, the closure rule's placement by the staged table), `chord/nextnode/plugins` (`node/destination.mjs`, `node/resolve.mjs`, `RuntimeLoad.ParentServes`), `chord/nextnode/app` (`start`). Nothing changes for a host without a delegate, or a NodeHost without `ParentServes`.

Owner decision: owner approved 2026-11-08 (17:29Z).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: never for the two hosts; the principal difference goes when a child's link can name whom a hold acts for.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/host/host_host.go` (`Delegate`, `Serves`), `chord/nextnode/app/app.go` (`start`), `chord/nextnode/plugins/node/destination.mjs`, `chord/next/host/host_delegate_test.go`.

Evidence: `TestDelegateServesTheUnionOfTheHostsTypes...`, `TestDelegateWhatTheDelegatesOwnLinkAsksForAndNothingServesIsNotSentBackToIt`, `TestDelegateWhenItIsDownItsTypesWaitAndTheHostsOwnAreUntouched`, `TestDelegateAHoldMadeWhileItIsDownWaitsOnTheResolvesErrorAndBindsOnceItIsBack`, `TestDelegateHoldsKeepTheirOrderAcrossTheDelegatesRestart...`, `TestDelegateIsRefusedFor...`, `TestDelegateTheClosureRuleReadsTheStagedTable...`, `TestHostServesTheTypesOfItsPublishedTableAndNoOtherName` (`chord/next/host/host_delegate_test.go`), `TestAServerPluginsObjectHoldsTheTypesTheGoServerServesOverTheNodeHostsLinkAsTheServer` (`chord/nextnode/app/app_delegate_test.go`: binds, calls, the principal difference), and the server rows of the reload suite (`chord/nextnode/app/reload_*_test.go`).

Parity allowance: none.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

## D607 The plugins component loads hosts through a Node subprocess it starts: Pi's `evaluate`, `stack` and runtime run in that subprocess, not in the Go process

What: Pi's plugin runtime (`packages/chord/src/node/plugins/runtime.ts`, `plugins.ts`) is handed a live `Host` and evaluates each plugin script in the same process (`evaluateFacet` with `vm.Script`, `mapStack` through a registry of source maps, `createPluginRuntime()` taking nothing). PiG's plugins are JavaScript and a Go process cannot run them, so Pi's files run verbatim in a Node subprocess (`chord/nextnode/plugins/node/pi/chord/src/node/plugins/`) and the Go side keeps what is data: `PluginRuntime.Load/Reload` and `Plugins.Load/Reload` start the subprocess host themselves (`NodeHost`) and take the options of that start (`RuntimeLoad.Core` is a module file, `Modules` are module paths) where Pi takes a `Host`, core `PluginFacet`s and live module values. `RolloutStep.Reload` also receives `applied`, the set the rollout left (Pi reads `plugins.current(kind)` atomically after its `ship`). `evaluateFacet`, `EvaluateOptions`, `BuiltScript`, `mapStack` and `HostPackages` have no Go form: Go sees the evaluation's failure as `*NodeError` with its `Stack`.

Why: a JavaScript facet, module instance or `Host` cannot cross the process boundary, and a second evaluator of plugins in the Go process is forbidden (see D502, D605).

Scope: `chord/nextnode/plugins` (`plugins_runtime.go`, `plugins_plugins.go`, `plugins_node*.go`, `node/`), `chord/nextnode/surfaces` (surfaces build after the server's listen, D462).

Owner decision: APPROVED for 0.8.0 (owner, 2026-11-08 17:29Z): the live-JS rows evaluateFacet, EvaluateOptions, BuiltScript, mapStack and HostPackages stay under D607 with their intention checks; follow-up after 0.8.0: Load takes a host as Pi does. SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_runtime.go` (`PluginRuntime`, `RuntimeLoad`), `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_plugins.go` (`Plugins.Load`, `Plugins.Step`, `RolloutStep`).

Evidence: `evaluate.test.ts`, `stack.test.ts` and `runtime.test.ts` ported through the subprocess (`plugins_evaluate_test.go`, `plugins_stack_test.go`, `plugins_runtime_test.go`); `plugins.test.ts` (per plugin at startup and reload, rollout, current sets, the app's API and required roots, fix round 1, GC) through `RuntimeLoad` and the Node host (`plugins_plugins_test.go`, `plugins_endtoend_test.go`, examples 35 and 38 in `plugins_examples_test.go`). The designed-out rows `PluginRuntime`/`Plugins` load, reload and step, `RuntimeLoad` (both packages) and `RolloutStep` carry the intention check in `test/parity/interface-closure/autobind/reviewed-decisions.json`.

Parity allowance: none beyond the forms above.

Remove when: PiG gets an in-process evaluator of plugins or Pi evaluates plugins out of process.

## D608 A client surface is built after the server's listen: `NewWebSurface`/`NewTuiSurface` return at once and `Build` builds the base bundle, where Pi's `webSurface`/`tuiSurface` await it

What: Pi's `webSurface(server, options, context)` and `tuiSurface(...)` (`packages/chord/src/node/surfaces/web.ts`, `tui.ts`) build the base bundle and take the kind's first set before they resolve, and the app awaits them before the server's load; the result carries `base` and `inputs`. PiG's `NewWebSurface` and `NewTuiSurface` (`chord/nextnode/surfaces`) return the surface at once and its `Build` runs after the server's listen (owner decision "A" of the kit, `chord/nextnode/app/app_bases.go`: nothing is kept between starts, the server answers meanwhile and each request needing the base waits for it; a failed build answers 503 and is reported `base_build`). The base and the inputs are read from `Build`'s result (`SurfaceBuilt`) or `Built`. The facet is two Go facets (`Facet`, `Scripts`), as a Go facet is opaque.

Why: the first set is published to the scripts object of the loaded server host (`PublishTo`), which needs the host loaded; Pi's facet publishes from its own `setup`.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/surfaces` (`surfaces_web.go`, `surfaces_tui.go`, `surfaces_build.go`), `chord/nextnode/app` (`app_surfaces.go`, `app_bases.go`).

Owner decision: APPROVED for 0.8.0 (owner, 2026-11-08 17:29Z); follow-up after 0.8.0: eager-build surfaces, design back to the owner first. SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/surfaces/surfaces_web.go` (`NewWebSurface`), `chord/nextnode/surfaces/surfaces_tui.go` (`NewTuiSurface`).

Evidence: `chord/nextnode/surfaces/surfaces_build_test.go`, `chord/nextnode/app/app_web_browser_test.go`.

Parity allowance: none beyond the forms above.

Remove when: a Go facet can publish from its own setup (the first set before the host is served).
